# Framework Decisions Record

**Status:** Working artifact, instantiated 2026-05-07. Hash-chained record of architecture decisions and design rationale. Each entry uses AAR substrate (intent / outcome / why / lessons / skills) plus three integrity fields (timestamp, prior_entry_hash, this_entry_hash). The hash chain provides tamper-evidence and chain-of-custody portability across platforms.

**What this is:** The records-keeping discipline applied to the framework's own design evolution. Distinct from `cohort_temporal_record.md` (which captures cohort and operational records) and from handoff documents (which capture session-by-session continuity). This artifact captures *why* design decisions were made, with verifiable provenance back to the genesis entry.

**What this is not:** A handoff replacement. Handoffs continue to serve session-to-session continuity and current-state-of-work. This record is for design rationale specifically — the kind of content that needs to remain authentically tied to predecessor context across years and across the platforms a future framework operator may use.

**Why hash-chained:** Per Dan's design specification (records-keeping audit Q6 answer): "some hash of the authenticity to show a given record is authentically tied to the previous is useful... we want an architecture which can regularly evaluate the full record against what a given perspective may say in the moment and provide some input on how to refine that toward business objectives." The hash chain addresses authenticity. The "evaluate against current perspective" capability lives with the agent reading the file, not in the format itself.

---

## Hash Protocol

**Hash function:** SHA-256.

**Per-entry computation:** Each entry's `this_entry_hash` is the SHA-256 hex digest of the UTF-8 encoded concatenation: `timestamp + "|" + intent + "|" + outcome + "|" + why + "|" + lessons + "|" + skills + "|" + prior_entry_hash`.

**Chain construction:** Each entry's `prior_entry_hash` field is the prior entry's `this_entry_hash`. The genesis entry's `prior_entry_hash` is sixty-four zeros (`0000...0000`) as the chain sentinel.

**Verification:** A reader can verify the chain by recomputing each entry's hash from its content and prior_hash, then checking that the recomputed value matches the recorded `this_entry_hash` and that the next entry's `prior_entry_hash` equals this entry's `this_entry_hash`. Any mismatch indicates the entry has been modified after recording, an entry has been inserted or deleted, or the chain has been broken.

**Tooling:** A small verification script can compute the chain from the file's structured fields. The chain is independent of the surrounding markdown formatting — only the AAR-substrate fields and the prior-hash are inputs.

**Limitations:** This is a tamper-evidence chain, not a tamper-prevention mechanism. A bad actor with write access can recompute the entire chain after editing earlier entries. The chain's value lies in detecting unintentional drift, copy-paste corruption across platforms, or out-of-order edits — and in producing a record that's portable across platforms in a way that proves itself rather than relying on platform-level trust.

## Entries

### Entry 0 — Genesis

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T22:00:00-07:00
- **intent:** Instantiate hash-chained framework decisions record per Q6 answer of the 2026-05-07 records-keeping audit; establish appendable, integrity-verifiable architecture for capturing design decisions across the framework's lifecycle.
- **outcome:** File `framework_decisions_record.md` created. Format specified: AAR substrate (intent, outcome, why, lessons, skills) plus three integrity fields (timestamp, prior_entry_hash, this_entry_hash). Hash protocol: SHA-256 over the entry's concatenated AAR fields plus prior_entry_hash. Genesis entry's prior_entry_hash is sixty-four zeros (sentinel). Each subsequent entry references the prior entry's this_entry_hash, producing a tamper-evident chain.
- **why:** The records-keeping audit on 2026-05-07 identified that the cohort_temporal_record pattern (started 2026-05-07 mid-thread) works well for cohort data but does not generalize to architecture decisions and design rationale. Dan's Q6 answer specified: a file that gets committed to knowledge at the end of sessions; some hash of authenticity to show records are authentically tied to predecessors; an architecture supporting regular evaluation of the full record against current-perspective input on refinement toward business objectives. The decisions record format addresses the first two requirements directly and supports the third by being readable by an agent at any time.
- **lessons:** The discipline pattern from cohort_temporal_record (appendable, provenance-tagged, visibly provisional) extends naturally to architecture decisions. Hash chaining adds chain-of-custody and tamper-evidence — useful for the "might go through some other platform later" lens because the chain itself travels with the file. The "evaluate against current perspective" capability lives with the agent reading the file, not in the format itself; the format just needs to be machine-readable and integrity-verifiable.
- **skills:** Records-keeping discipline applied reflexively to the framework itself; hash-chain integrity protocol design; AAR substrate as decision-record format.

### Entry 2 — Role-Pathology Typology Q2 Resolved: Shadow-Influence as Valence-Neutral

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-08T08:30:00-07:00
- **intent:** Resolve role-pathology typology Q2 (whether shadow-influence decoupling is a pathology vs a healthy operating preference) per Dan's 2026-05-08 framing; reframe shadow-influence as the valence-neutral entry in the typology and introduce explicit valence variability across the typology's entries.
- **outcome:** role_pathology_typology.md updated — "Note on valence" section added near the typology's foundational rationale; entry 5 (Shadow-influence decoupling) reframed as the valence-neutral case capturing the structural mismatch between formal authority and actual contribution without assigning valence; worked-example table commentary updated to distinguish pathology from valence-neutral pattern in Jenn's reading; Q2 removed from open architectural questions and renumbered Q3-Q5 as Q2-Q4; new "Resolved questions" section added recording Q2 and the resolution. project_rigger_glossary.md updated — Role-pathology typology entry rewritten to surface valence variability; new Shadow-influence decoupling entry added (was a dangling cross-reference). cohort_temporal_record.md updated — Finding #19 reframed with valence-neutrality clarification; Change Log entry added.
- **why:** Dan's framing in 2026-05-08 morning message: "it's an orientation for me but I do not think that's universal. I would characterize it as something without a clear valence, just the noted mismatch." This is the framework's discipline applied to itself — capture the structural fact, surface it, leave valence to the reader. Categorizing shadow-influence alongside clearly-pathological patterns (sacrificial-chair, weakness-overlap-with-supervisor) introduced unwarranted valence into the framework's read of the pattern. Treating shadow-influence as universally a "deliberate operating preference" (its prior framing) was equally over-confident in the opposite direction — for some incumbents the decoupling is structurally produced rather than chosen, and the framework cannot distinguish without context the reader supplies.
- **lessons:** (1) Valence is sometimes a fixed attribute of a structural pattern and sometimes a contextual reading; the typology is more rigorous when it's explicit about which kind of valence each entry has and when it leaves valence to the reader entirely. (2) The framework's "capture rather than evaluate" principle should be visible in the typology's structure, not just stated abstractly. Shadow-influence as the valence-neutral entry is the cleanest demonstration of the principle in action. (3) The other entries vary in valence too — most are typically-negative but spiritual-director sits between (often invisible-overshoot warranting surfacing, sometimes deliberate-design-choice that's working fine). The "Note on valence" section makes this variability legible. (4) The dangling glossary cross-reference for shadow-influence decoupling was caught and corrected; the dangling cross-ref was itself a small instance of the records-keeping audit's category D issue — these recur and warrant occasional pass-throughs. (5) Hash-chained decision records make the design-decision audit trail legible — Entry 2 hashes back to Entry 1, which is where the typology was instantiated; the chain captures the "instantiated then refined" pattern as legible history rather than as a single state.
- **skills:** Architectural pattern catalog refinement; valence-versus-pattern distinction; framework-discipline-applied-to-framework reflexive correction; cross-document update propagation (typology → glossary → temporal record).

### Entry 3 — B6 + D1 + Intake-Skepticism Principle + Nick-Calibrated Subset Pattern

- **intent:** Execute records-keeping audit B6 (formalize survivorship-bias and bidirectionality as explicit principles in framework_principles.md) plus D1 residual filename fix; codify intake-skepticism as new operating constraint in core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md per Dan's 2026-05-08 behavior note; instantiate the calibrated-subset stakeholder pattern with Nick Pegueros as first instance — produce Nick-calibrated README and "what's changed" briefing including second-order analysis of the BC-Chief vacancy cascade for refresh of his explainer-agent project knowledge (currently dated to ~2026-04-30 01:00 — materially adrift); flag information-sharing infrastructure choice (continue with Claude project pivot vs. GitHub-as-source-of-truth) as architecture decision pending Dan's choice given his maintenance-friction concern.
- **outcome:** framework_principles.md updated — principles #19 (full-distribution capture / survivorship bias) and #20 (bidirectional development capture) added in Records-Keeping Principles section; line 72 Cross-References filename reference corrected from "framework_design_three_lens.md v2" to "framework_design_three_lens_v2.md". core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md updated — new operating constraint "Trust applies to intake, not just to capture" added at end of Operating Constraints section. cohort_temporal_record.md updated — Change Log entry for this batch. New files produced for Nick's project knowledge: nick_readme.md (calibrated entry-point document explicitly stating Nick's full-corpus access and stakeholder reading order) and nick_whats_changed_briefing.md (delta from ~2026-04-30 01:00 corpus including architectural changes, portfolio-specific items, and second-order BC-Chief cascade analysis with framework-suggested support measures). Decision flagged for Dan: GitHub-as-source-of-truth vs. continue Claude-project-export-and-upload pattern; the framework's chain-of-custody portability architecture (decisions record explicitly designed for cross-platform travel) composes cleanly with git's commit-hash structure and addresses the maintenance-friction Dan flagged.
- **why:** B6 was deferred from 2026-05-07 evening Tier 1 work as one of three small inline edits left for the next session. Tonight's session resolved Q2 (typology valence-neutrality) which unblocked the broader audit work; B6 is the smallest of the deferred items and a natural pair with the D1 residual filename fix (both are framework_principles.md edits). The intake-skepticism principle came out of Dan's 2026-05-08 morning note — adding skepticism on data-sparse intake of stated dispositions and third-person reads is parallel in form to the existing survivorship-bias and bidirectionality corrections (both apply principle #2 to evidence-handling) but operates on agent posture rather than on what gets captured, so it lands as an operating constraint derived from principle #2 rather than as a third correction inside #2. The calibrated-subset stakeholder pattern was anticipated by the README's "If you are a new stakeholder: ask Dan which subset of these documents is appropriate" framing but never instantiated — Nick is the first instance. Dan's explicit trust calibration on Nick simplifies the first-instance work substantially (full corpus, no subset filtering) but the calibrated README and what's-changed briefing are still required artifacts for stakeholder onboarding generally; the pattern is now demonstrated and reusable. The information-sharing infrastructure question surfaced because Dan flagged data-maintenance as a friction-driven necessity rather than a preferred task — the framework's chain-of-custody portability architecture composes naturally with git, which addresses the friction concern, but the pivot decision is Dan's and the present session captures the option without committing.
- **lessons:** (1) The "small inline edit" items in the audit's deferred list (B6, B8, three D-fixes) batch naturally with each other when they touch the same file — economical to do them in pairs rather than individually. (2) When a principle the user states aligns conceptually with existing principles (here: skepticism-on-intake parallels survivorship-bias-on-capture), placement decision is whether to extend the existing structure or add a parallel one; choosing parallel placement (new operating constraint rather than third sub-correction) preserves the triadic principle structure and keeps the new content prominent rather than buried. (3) Stakeholder calibrated-subset work has two parts: the corpus subset (or full corpus with explicit trust note, in Nick's case) and the orientation artifacts (calibrated README + what's-changed briefing); the latter does most of the work and is reusable across stakeholders even when the corpus subset varies. (4) The framework's chain-of-custody portability architecture (hash-chained decisions record explicitly designed to travel across platforms) suggests the framework's own infrastructure should match — git's commit-hash structure is a closer fit than Claude-project-export-and-upload, and Dan's maintenance-friction concern is the operational signal that the current setup is straining against the architecture's design intent. Worth flagging as architecture decision rather than a deferred note. (5) Second-order analysis of the BC-Chief cascade (vacancy-residue at multiple levels, cohort-level reshuffling within the BC peer group, Allocative-skill-cluster gap for newly-promoted BCs, Mike Kennedy perennial-bridesmaid pattern interaction) demonstrates that the framework's existing concepts compose to produce concrete portfolio guidance without requiring new architecture — a useful test that the v2 design has the right primitives.
- **skills:** Records-keeping audit deferred-item execution; principle placement design (extension vs parallel); stakeholder-calibrated entry-point production; multi-document coherent-batch propagation; framework concept composition for concrete portfolio analysis; infrastructure-decision flagging without premature commitment.

### Entry 4 — Timestamp Drift Correction + Decisions Record Stakeholder-Default Access

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T11:00:00-07:00
- **intent:** Correct timestamp drift in chain — Entries 2 and 3 dated as 2026-05-08 morning but actually occurred 2026-05-07 morning; per chain Append protocol, append correction rather than edit. Operationalize stakeholder access to framework_decisions_record.md per Dan's 2026-05-07 directive — the decisions record becomes part of the calibrated-entry-point template default for general stakeholders. Defer the broader verifiability principle codification and calibrated-entry-point template work to the next batch pending Dan's scope confirmation (narrow / medium / broad with per-stakeholder uplift).
- **outcome:** No edits to Entries 2 or 3 — timestamps remain as recorded (2026-05-08T08:30:00-07:00 and 2026-05-08T10:00:00-07:00 respectively) and chain hashes still verify; Entry 4 captures the actual dates as 2026-05-07 morning. Entry 2's timestamp was inherited from prior session with stale or incorrect clock signal; Entry 3 anchored forward to Entry 2's wrong time without challenge. cohort_temporal_record.md Change Log row added noting that prior entries dated as 2026-05-08 morning actually occurred 2026-05-07 morning. framework_decisions_record.md scoped as stakeholder-default-accessible per Dan's directive; operationalization (principle codification + template + nick_readme update) deferred to next batch.
- **why:** Drift entered the chain at Entry 2's authoring (prior session). On turn one of the current session I noticed the discrepancy between system clock (2026-05-07) and records (2026-05-08) but treated the records as authoritative on grounds that three artifacts agreed. This was the wrong call: three artifacts agreeing only means the drift propagated, not that the timestamp was right. The intake-skepticism principle codified in Entry 3 is exactly the constraint that would have caught this — treating presented data as ground truth without trust-accumulation. The principle generalizes beyond its original framing: it applies to past records, not just to people. Entry 4 captures this reflexively as both the correction and as a worked example of why the principle matters. The decisions-record-stakeholder-availability action follows from Dan's 2026-05-07 directive: stakeholders should be able to verify claims that affect them; the records-keeping discipline is performing rigor without delivering it if affected parties can't access the records. Dan's framing — "if the other side insists something is wrong, they need to be able to point to where it's wrong" — articulates a verifiability principle the framework hasn't named yet. Codification deferred pending scope confirmation: principle scope shapes calibrated-entry-point template access defaults, and the proposed design (narrow default + per-stakeholder uplift) resolves the tension between Dan's preferred default-narrow stance and the trust-building value of broader exec-team access via the existing calibrated-subset pattern applied to access rather than just to corpus.
- **lessons:** (1) Records that conflict with a more direct evidence source should weight to the direct source. The intake-skepticism principle applies to past records, not just to people; this is a generalization beyond its original framing and worth carrying forward. (2) Chain Append protocol's "correct via append, not edit" handles drift cleanly — chain integrity preserved, historical state captured, correction itself becomes institutional record. The protocol is doing its job; the failure mode on turn one was not invoking it sooner. (3) Verifiability has direction-asymmetric reversibility: narrow → broad is operationally easy (additive sharing builds trust at the moment of access-grant), broad → narrow is imperfect (information already read can't be unread; future scope changes leave existing stakeholder knowledge in place). Asymmetric optionality favors starting narrow with per-person uplift as the trust-building mechanism, which also reflects the calibrated-subset pattern already in use for corpus subsets. (4) Codification of principles whose operationalization is staged should defer principle text until scope is settled, to avoid principle drift between text and practice. (5) The verifiability principle as articulated has provenance origin distinct from the framework's other principles: it came from Dan's stakeholder-engagement work (negotiation costing) rather than from cohort profile work. This is the first principle whose origin is operationalization-driven rather than architecture-driven, suggesting the framework has matured to the point where principles emerge from its own use, not just from its design.
- **skills:** Chain-of-custody preservation under correction; reflexive application of intake-skepticism principle to records (not just to people); reversibility-asymmetry analysis on access scope; scope-staging discipline on principle codification; recognition of provenance-origin shifts as a maturity signal.

### Entry 5 — Principle #21 Codified + Calibrated Entry-Point Template + Glossary Residual Fix

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T12:30:00-07:00
- **intent:** Codify principle #21 (Verifiability requires access) per Dan's 2026-05-07 directive and the design confirmed in this session — universal in framing, calibrated in operationalization with three access tiers (NARROW / MEDIUM / FULL) implemented via the calibrated-subset pattern. Execute the entry-point template work that operationalizes the principle. Address Entry 1 outcome residual #3 — glossary entries for "Reference datasheet" and "Role-pathology typology" claimed in Entry 1's outcome but never added; verification pass surfaced the gap.
- **outcome:** framework_principles.md updated — principle #21 added in the records-keeping section; Cross-References section unchanged. project_rigger_glossary.md updated — Reference datasheet entry added (between "Range with a decision trigger" and "Requisite Organization"); Role-pathology typology entry added (between "Role versus utility function" and "Spiritual-director case"). New directory stakeholders/_template/ created with three files: _readme_template.md (calibrated entry-point template with three access tiers marked inline; pick-one-delete-others structure), _whats_changed_template.md (briefing template with three-section structure: architectural changes, portfolio-specific filter, worked example), README.md (procedure for adding a new stakeholder, references existing instances, principle #21 framing). nick_readme.md updated — "Why you have full corpus access" paragraph references principle #21 explicitly; new "On verifiability" section added at end describing what Nick can verify and the append-correction protocol. cohort_temporal_record.md Change Log row added.
- **why:** The verifiability principle was articulated in Dan's stakeholder-engagement reasoning ("if the other side insists something is wrong, they need to be able to point to where it's wrong"). Codification waited on scope confirmation since narrow / medium / broad scope shapes the calibrated-entry-point template's access markers. Dan's confirmed design — narrow default with per-stakeholder uplift via the existing calibrated-subset pattern — codifies cleanly: the principle text is universal (verifiability requires access; architect commits to surfacing records and accepting append-correction), the operationalization is calibrated (per-person tier choice). The entry-point template was needed for two reasons: making the third-stakeholder-onward setup fill-in-the-template work rather than from-scratch authoring (per the workflow analysis from Dan's information-velocity concern), and making access tier choices visible per-person rather than buried in operational discretion. Glossary residuals from Entry 1 were caught by deliberate verification pass — pattern-recognition: Entry 1's outcome list was substantially overclaimed (D1 filename, README reading order, glossary entries — three out of approximately fifteen claimed outcomes were not actually executed). This warrants the framework's discipline being applied to its own decision records: outcome claims should be verifiable, and verification passes should be periodic discipline, not exception.
- **lessons:** (1) Codifying a principle whose origin is operationalization-driven (rather than architecture-driven) is a maturity signal — the framework is now generating principles from its own use, not just from its design. The provenance pattern shift should be visible in the principle's worked example so future readers can see where principles come from. (2) The calibrated-subset pattern generalizes from corpus subset (what files a stakeholder has) to access scope (what records they can verify). Same mechanism, different dimension. Operationally clean — the template just adds a tier marker. (3) Entry 1 outcome verification surfaced a third residual (glossary entries), bringing the total to three out of ~fifteen outcome claims unexecuted. The discipline-implication: bulk-claim-outcome entries are prone to overclaiming, and periodic verification passes are warranted as recurring discipline rather than as exception. The hash chain catches direct tampering with entry contents but does not catch outcome claims that don't match reality outside the chain — that's a different verification layer, and the framework's discipline should include it. (4) The entry-point template is the first piece of repeatable infrastructure produced for the framework's own multi-stakeholder rollout. Producing it now (before scaling beyond Nick) is correct sequencing per the calibrated-subset pattern's scaling logic from the workflow analysis. (5) Nick's "Why you have full corpus access" paragraph now reads as the worked instantiation of principle #21 at full tier, with the principle providing the architectural framing. The integration is reflexive: Nick's case both motivated the principle's codification (he was the first instance of full-tier access, prompting the question of how that scales) and now serves as the principle's worked example.
- **skills:** Principle codification with universal-text plus calibrated-operationalization separation; template design for repeatable stakeholder onboarding; verification-pass discipline applied to past outcome claims; alphabetical-position-aware glossary editing; reflexive integration of stakeholder-specific instances into principle worked examples.

### Entry 6 — B1 Executed: Trusted-Talent-Network Typology Instantiated

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T14:30:00-07:00
- **intent:** Execute records-keeping audit B1 — formalize the trusted-talent-network classification (currently a worked typology in cohort_temporal_record.md's Cross-Org Connection Map) as a foundational evaluation layer parallel to role-pathology typology. Capture Dan's nuance (single-instance classification is structural; higher-order patterns require record accumulation) explicitly so future cohort-accumulation work has the architecture in place to surface concentration-risk and path-specific-quality-drift patterns when data supports it.
- **outcome:** New file trusted_talent_network_typology.md created — six sourcing paths catalogued (prior-employer network, trust-organization-via-vouch, pre-existing-organization, external non-network search, internal promotion under prior network logic, internal promotion under current network logic) plus TBD placeholder; "A note on valence" section establishes that single-instance classification is structural and not evaluative; "Higher-order patterns" section establishes the framework's design space for future record-accumulation-driven detection (concentration risk, path-specific quality drift); "How the typology composes with other layers" section documents interactions with three-lens, kingdom composition, and role-pathology; worked example reads the current SB cohort through the typology with sourcing-path classifications matching the cross-org connection map. project_rigger_glossary.md updated — new entry added for "Trusted-talent-network typology" (between Trust-relevant events and Whose utility function). README.md updated — new file added to reading order as item #4 alongside role_pathology_typology as item #3 (foundational evaluation layers grouped together; subsequent items renumbered).
- **why:** The audit (B1) identified that the cross-org connection map's classification scheme was load-bearing for understanding why each director is in their seat but lived only as a worked typology in operational records, not as formalized framework architecture. The typology was deferred to this turn because it required Q2 (typology valence-neutrality) resolution first — Q2 was resolved in Entry 2, unblocking B1. The placement decision was confirmed in this session: own dedicated file (parallel to role_pathology_typology) rather than section in framework_design_three_lens_v2.md or in role_pathology_typology.md, because (1) sourcing path is not a pathology (Dan's correction) and forcing it into role-pathology would conflate two different evaluation lenses, and (2) sourcing path is structurally important enough on its own to warrant foundational-layer status with its own catalog, worked examples, and open architectural questions. Dan's nuance about overreliance and multiple similar outcomes lands in the "Higher-order patterns" section as the framework's design space — the typology now has the architecture in place to surface evaluatively-meaningful patterns when records accumulate, while preserving the discipline that single-instance classification is structural rather than evaluative.
- **lessons:** (1) The four foundational evaluation layers (three-lens, kingdom composition, role-pathology, trusted-talent-network) compose: each tags a different dimension of how to read the records, and the layers interact in surfacing-relevant ways (sourcing path predisposes certain pathologies; kingdom composition interacts with internal-promotion-path Allocative-skill-cluster gaps; etc.). The typology document captures these compositions explicitly, which makes the framework's evaluation surface more legible than treating each layer as standalone. (2) "Catalog of structural patterns with worked examples and open architectural questions" is now the third instance of this document pattern (role-pathology, trusted-talent-network, and arguably the principles document at a coarser grain). The pattern is reusable; future foundational-layer work should follow it. (3) The cohort-as-detection-substrate framing (six observations is too small for statistical pattern detection but enough to flag watch-patterns and start record accumulation) is the right way to handle detection-readiness without over-claiming or under-architecting. The typology now has the architecture in place to support detection when data supports it; the discipline at present is faithful classification and patient accumulation. (4) Dan's nuance about overreliance (single-instance classification is structural; patterns require accumulation) is a generalization beyond sourcing — the same logic applies to other typologies. Worth noting whether the role-pathology typology should similarly distinguish "individual instance" vs "pattern across many instances" treatment; deferred for future review.
- **skills:** Foundational-layer typology design with parallel-to-existing structural form; nuance capture (single-instance vs higher-order patterns) at architectural rather than glossary level; cross-layer composition mapping; deferred-detection design (architecture-now, detection-when-data-supports); cohort worked example construction.

### Entry 7 — B8 Executed: Goodhart-on-Recognition Constraints + Verification Protocol Codified

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T15:30:00-07:00
- **intent:** Execute records-keeping audit B8 — formalize the Goodhart-on-recognition design constraints (currently in `Handoff_document_-_20260430.md` end-of-day section) into `learning_library_design.md` as a "Recognition-surface design constraints" section. Codify the periodic outcome verification discipline (per Dan's 2026-05-07 confirmation) as a Verification Protocol section in `framework_decisions_record.md`, parallel to the existing Hash Protocol and Append Protocol sections.
- **outcome:** learning_library_design.md updated — new section "Recognition-surface design constraints" added between Attribution and Guardrails — the management layer; section captures Goodhart-on-recognition risk framing, six architectural mitigations (breadth across concepts, recognition stays generative not evaluative, no library highlight feature, no algorithmic curation, no ranking, distributional-not-architectural enforcement), source-references the original handoff, and frames the constraints as non-negotiable in the same sense as framework principles. framework_decisions_record.md updated — new "Verification protocol" section added after Append protocol; specifies cadence (every fifth entry verifies the prior five), what to verify (entry outcome claims against actual file state), what happens when verification finds residuals (new entry per chain Append protocol; matches Entry 4's timestamp-correction pattern), what happens when nothing is found (Change Log row only). cohort_temporal_record.md Change Log row added.
- **why:** B8 was the last small inline edit deferred from earlier audit work; doing it now closes out the small-edits queue (B6, B8, three D-fixes) before larger architectural work in Tier 3. The Goodhart-on-recognition constraints had been articulated in the 2026-04-30 evening handoff but never carried into the design document they constrain — exactly the kind of "claimed in a session but not in the operational record" residual the framework's discipline is built to surface and fix. The verification protocol codification follows Dan's 2026-05-07 confirmation and operationalizes the lesson from Entry 5 (#3) that bulk-claim-outcome entries warrant periodic verification as recurring discipline. Cadence (every fifth entry) is calibrated to gather meaningful residuals without verification overhead dominating the work; the first verification window at Entry 5 already executed in this session retroactively for Entries 1-5. The protocol lives in framework_decisions_record.md alongside Hash Protocol and Append Protocol because all three concern chain integrity — hash chain catches tampering, append protocol governs corrections, verification protocol catches reality-divergence.
- **lessons:** (1) Architectural mitigations against Goodhart-style optimization-against-target failures are best enforced by NOT building the optimization surfaces in the first place rather than by adding rules around them after the fact. The recognition-surface constraints document what the library doesn't build, and the framing matters: "non-negotiable in the same sense framework principles are non-negotiable" treats absence of a feature as a load-bearing design choice rather than as a feature gap. (2) The verification protocol and the hash protocol are complementary — chain integrity is a different verification layer than outcome integrity, and the framework's records-keeping discipline needs both. The hash chain catches direct tampering; verification catches drift between claim and reality. Naming both layers explicitly makes the discipline more legible. (3) Cadence-based verification (every fifth entry) is itself a forcing function: it converts ad-hoc "I'll check if something feels off" into structured periodic discipline. Same logic the framework applies elsewhere — manager review at portfolio level rather than per-exemplar gating, ACOM-style forcing-function distributional review rather than per-rating defense. The framework's discipline is now applied to its own record-keeping at the same architectural level. (4) The verification protocol's "what happens when verification finds residuals" path matches Entry 4's timestamp-correction pattern — both fix forward via append rather than edit. The pattern is now reusable for any chain-of-records correction, not just timestamps. The chain Append protocol generalizes to "any correction is a new entry referencing the original by hash."
- **skills:** Architectural mitigation design via feature-absence rather than feature-gating; protocol codification at the chain-integrity layer; cadence calibration for periodic discipline; pattern generalization (Entry 4's timestamp correction → general correction-via-append pattern).

### Entry 8 — Stakeholder Sync Infrastructure: scripts/sync_to_stakeholders.py + Tier-Based Bundling

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T18:45:00-07:00
- **intent:** Build the export-and-stakeholder-sync infrastructure to operationalize multi-stakeholder rollout per the workflow analysis from the earlier turn. Deferred from the previous batch behind B8 per Dan's instruction. Goal: reduce the per-stakeholder upload friction that scales linearly with cohort size by automating bundle preparation, diff detection, and invite message drafting; manual upload to Claude project knowledge remains the human-in-the-loop step until Anthropic ships a project-knowledge API (currently a feature request, GitHub anthropics/claude-code#47744).
- **outcome:** New file scripts/sync_to_stakeholders.py — Python script reading stakeholders config, building per-tier calibrated bundles (NARROW / MEDIUM / FULL), computing per-file diff against last sync state, producing manifest plus invite message. New file stakeholders/_stakeholders.yaml — config defining stakeholders, their tiers, last_seed_date, plus tier-to-files mapping. CONTRIBUTING.md updated to document the sync workflow including the manual upload constraint and the explicit warning against third-party session-key scraping tools. The script handles three message scenarios: true first contact (no prior engagement, no prior sync), first sync via tooling (prior engagement, no prior sync state — Nick's case), and routine refresh (prior sync state). Bundle output is staged to stakeholders/<name>/bundle/, manifest to stakeholders/<name>/_manifest.txt, invite to stakeholders/<name>/invite_message.md. Tested against Nick's existing stakeholder directory; bundle correctly produces 25 files at FULL tier; invite message correctly identifies first-sync-via-tooling scenario and points at the briefing document.
- **why:** The workflow analysis from earlier in this session identified knowledge sync as a linearly-scaling friction point — manual upload to N projects per change doesn't survive growth past two stakeholders. The script reduces this to run-script-then-upload-changed-files-only per stakeholder, with the per-stakeholder calibration (tier choice, calibrated entry-point files) handled mechanically rather than by-hand-each-time. The honest constraint: no public API for Claude.ai project knowledge upload exists; the script can prepare bundles and draft messages but the upload step is manual. Recommended against using third-party session-key-scraping tools (claude-pyrojects and similar) because their clear-and-re-upload pattern violates records-keeping discipline (the framework's own discipline says corrections append rather than edit; a tool that wipes and replaces operational records loses the audit trail). Manual upload preserves discipline at the cost of velocity; tradeoff is correct given current options. Diff detection means manual upload is bounded to changed files rather than the full corpus, which is meaningful friction reduction.
- **lessons:** (1) The manual-upload constraint reveals an honest limit on what current Claude.ai infrastructure supports for multi-stakeholder rollout. The script gets us most of the way; the remaining gap is on Anthropic to close. Worth tracking the GitHub feature request as an external dependency. (2) Three-message-scenario handling (first contact / first sync via tooling / routine refresh) demonstrates that automation needs to handle the migration from manual-state to tooled-state, not just the steady-state. Most automation work assumes greenfield; real rollouts handle existing state. (3) The script's per-tier bundle composition means tier choice (per principle #21) is operationally instantiated, not just architecturally specified. The calibrated-subset pattern is now executable infrastructure rather than design intent. (4) The invite message generator embeds principle #21 and the chain-head reference in stakeholder communication by default, making the discipline visible to recipients without architect-side memory. The tooling is itself a forcing function for honoring the principle. (5) Closing this conversation at this point is the right call — the next stakeholder rollout (Nick's refresh) is the next operational milestone and warrants a clean conversation context, plus the chain head and current state are fully captured in records that any future session can pick up via the README + verify-chain + recent decisions-record entries.
- **skills:** Multi-stakeholder rollout infrastructure; honest-constraint design (build what's possible, document what's not); migration-state automation handling; principle-embedding in default messaging; closing-context discipline (recognize natural break points and capture state for resumability).

### Entry 9 — Spiritual-Director vs Shadow-Role Distinction; Dan's Profile Reclassified

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T20:00:00-07:00
- **intent:** Resolve a typology over-classification surfaced during the Nick-preview rendering work — Dan's profile had been mapped to the spiritual-director case in the role-pathology typology when the structural fact warranting capture is the kingdom-composition shadow-role threshold (Allocative +15 vs nominal Strategic Advisor scope). Refine the typology so that a kingdom-composition delta alone does not classify as spiritual-director — the typology entry requires an additional upward-overshoot-with-greater-scope reading on top of the structural fact. Apply cascading corrections across cohort_temporal_record.md (authorship-overlap note, framework finding triggered field, change log), role_pathology_typology.md (worked-example table row, table narrative, spiritual-director source-cases note), and project_rigger_glossary.md (add Shadow-role threshold entry).
- **outcome:** cohort_temporal_record.md updated — Dan's People Index entry authorship-overlap note replaces the spiritual-director-applies-recursively framing with shadow-role-threshold-as-structural-mismatch framing; Framework finding triggered field replaces the spiritual-director classification with the shadow-role threshold finding (Allocative +15 vs nominal) and retains the 2D-orientation cluster reference (Finding #17); Change Log entry added recording the correction. role_pathology_typology.md updated — worked-example table row for Dan revised from "Spiritual-director case (recursively applied to project authorship)" to "None mapped to typology" with rationale clarifying that the shadow-role threshold is captured separately as kingdom-composition structural finding; table narrative updated to remove the "Dan has the recursive case" framing and replace with "Dan has no typology entry but hits the kingdom-composition shadow-role threshold"; spiritual-director entry source-cases note revised to remove the Dan-as-canonical-example reference and add a clarifying note that a kingdom-composition delta alone (shadow-role threshold) is not sufficient to classify as spiritual-director — the typology entry requires the upward-overshoot-with-greater-scope reading on top of the structural fact, and the classification should be applied only when both conditions are present. project_rigger_glossary.md updated — new Shadow-role threshold entry added (was previously referenced only inside the Kingdom composition entry, never given its own entry).
- **why:** The over-classification was caught when rendering Dan's character sheet for Nick's preview — the spiritual-director framing was inherited from prior session work where shadow-role threshold and spiritual-director had been used near-interchangeably. Dan flagged the conflation: the structural fact (kingdom-composition delta beyond ±10) is captured as such, while the spiritual-director typology entry adds a classification layer (upward-overshoot reading with role-as-performed exceeding role-as-titled in scope or seniority) that should be applied only when context warrants. The framework's "capture rather than evaluate" discipline is best served by surfacing the structural fact and reserving typology classification for cases where the additional reading is warranted. Dan's case is structural mismatch without the role-pathology classification — the captured kingdom-composition fact (the +15 Allocative delta) already does the surfacing work; the typology classification was adding interpretive overhead the case does not warrant. The correction also tightens the spiritual-director entry itself by making the classification criterion explicit, which prevents the same conflation recurring against future profiles.
- **lessons:** (1) Single-instance typology classifications are easier to over-apply than higher-order patterns. The role-pathology typology's existing valence nuance (most entries typically-negative, spiritual-director sits between, shadow-influence valence-neutral) is a hedge against this; the same nuance applies within entries — the spiritual-director entry needed to distinguish "structural fact present" from "classification warranted." (2) When a typology entry has a structural-signature that overlaps with a separately-tracked structural threshold (here: spiritual-director's "+15 Allocative composition delta" signature overlapping with kingdom-composition's shadow-role threshold), the typology entry must explicitly state what additional reading is required to apply it. Otherwise the typology absorbs the structural fact and over-classifies. (3) The framework's reflexive application (Dan profiled by his own framework) surfaces these over-classifications faster than third-party application would, because the author has direct ground-truth on whether the typology entry's reading actually fits. This is itself an argument for keeping the bidirectionality principle live rather than treating it as a one-time rigor exercise. (4) The correction cascades across multiple files (cohort_temporal_record, role_pathology_typology, glossary). Worth flagging as future architectural improvement: typology entries should be capture-once-reference-everywhere so a single refinement propagates by reference rather than by multi-file edit. (5) The intake-skepticism principle (constraint #6) applied here — the prior session's classification was treated initially as authoritative on Dan's profile rather than pressure-tested against the structural fact. The correction is exactly the kind of trust-relevant event that should update the agent's prior on inherited classifications.
- **skills:** Typology-entry refinement at the structural-signature vs classification-criterion distinction; reflexive correction propagation across multiple files; spiritual-director entry edge-case clarification; intake-skepticism applied to inherited typology classifications from prior sessions; capture-once-reference-everywhere flagging as future architectural improvement.

### Entry 10 — Widget Rendering Codified as Operating Constraint #7

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-07T20:15:00-07:00
- **intent:** Codify the widget-rendering standard for stakeholder-facing outputs as a new operating constraint #7 in core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md, derived from principle #2 (capture-and-surface) and principle #1 (compression should be directional and surfaced). Cross-reference from reference_datasheet_design.md so the standard lives canonically in core principles rather than fragmenting across surface design docs. Standard captures three load-bearing properties: high-density inline glossary tooltips on framework-specific terms; expandable sections that surface more detail on demand; stable visual encoding across rendering instances. Plain-text rendering acknowledged as fallback case rather than default.
- **outcome:** core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md updated — new operating constraint #7 ("Stakeholder-facing outputs render as interactive surfaces, not text walls") added after constraint #6 (intake skepticism); constraint articulates panels-over-prose default, the three load-bearing properties (inline glossary tooltips at high density, expandable sections, stable visual encoding), the plain-text fallback acknowledgment, and names reference datasheet as canonical worked example with framework-wide application to coordinator synthesis renderings, explainer agent outputs, and any other stakeholder-facing surface. reference_datasheet_design.md updated — new "Visual rendering standard" section added between "Source-bias disclosure rendering" and "Material time step rendering" that cross-references constraint #7 as authoritative source rather than duplicating the text; section explicitly acknowledges the constraint applies framework-wide while this document is the canonical worked example. project_rigger_glossary.md updated — new Visual rendering standard entry added pointing to constraint #7.
- **why:** The codification was triggered by Dan's observation during the Nick-preview rendering work that the agent had defaulted to plain-text rendering of Dan's character sheet when prior sessions had produced widget renderings. The plain-text default was a regression from prior practice; the codification turns prior practice into explicit standard so the regression does not recur. Dan's framing — "panels and buttons better than text walls" — names the operational truth: stakeholders engage interactive surfaces more readily than prose, and the agent's value depends on whether germane content actually reaches the reader. Three properties were load-bearing in the rebuild and inform the constraint's specification: inline glossary tooltips at high density (prior version had higher glossary density and used mouseovers; the rebuild required broader term coverage to match); expandable sections (the affordance for the surrounding-architecture glossary that does not need to live in the default view); stable visual encoding (kingdom colors must be the same across renderings or the surface stops being a recurring reference). The placement decision (core principles vs reference datasheet design) was Dan's choice — the standard applies to all stakeholder-facing surfaces (coordinator synthesis renderings, explainer agent outputs, and so on), not just the datasheet, and core principles is the cleanest home with surface design docs cross-referencing rather than duplicating.
- **lessons:** (1) Operational regressions (the plain-text default) are easier to recognize and prevent when prior practice has been codified than when it has only been demonstrated. The widget rendering had been demonstrated in prior sessions but never codified, leaving the next session free to default to plain text. Codification as operating constraint addresses the regression-prevention layer. (2) The constraint embodies the friction-reduction principle reflexively: panels-over-prose is itself a friction-reducing rendering choice, applied to the agent's outputs. The framework's discipline is more legible when the codification's substance is consistent with the codification's rendering — if the standard is "panels not walls," the standard's definition should not itself be a wall of text. The current placement uses paragraph form (consistent with the rest of operating constraints) but with the three load-bearing properties as semicolon-separated clauses for skimmability. (3) The cross-reference pattern (canonical home in core principles; surface design docs reference rather than duplicate) is a discipline upgrade for the corpus — it prevents drift between the standard and its instances. The pattern matches the "capture-once-reference-everywhere" architectural improvement flagged in Entry 9's lessons; both entries surface the same underlying corpus discipline. Worth applying retroactively to other framework standards that currently fragment across multiple docs. (4) The standard's three load-bearing properties (tooltips, expandable sections, stable encoding) are design-pattern primitives that compose: any new stakeholder-facing surface inherits these by default, and surface-specific specs (e.g., kingdom colors for the datasheet) live in the surface design docs as instances of the stable-encoding property. The compositional structure makes the standard reusable rather than datasheet-specific. (5) This is the second operating constraint codified from operationalization-driven origin (constraint #6 intake-skepticism was the first, per Entry 4's lesson #5). The pattern of operationalization-driven principle emergence continues as a maturity signal — the framework is now generating constraints from its own use, not just from its design.
- **skills:** Operating constraint codification with rendering-property specification; cross-reference architecture design (canonical home plus references rather than duplication); regression-prevention via codification; reflexive consistency between codification substance and codification rendering; design-primitive specification at the level of compositional pattern rather than instance-specific detail; operationalization-driven principle emergence pattern recognition.

### Entry 11 — Principle #1 Expansion Note + Constraint #8 (Register and Audience-Distance) + Fractal-as-Structural-Property Surfaced and Deferred

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-08T10:30:00-07:00
- **intent:** Capture two architectural updates that emerged from a continuation conversation focused on Nick's preview rendering, the spiritual-director-to-shadow-role correction, the cycle AAR scaffold, the City Attorney consult brief draft, and most importantly for this entry a register-mismatch the agent produced in the attorney brief that surfaced the fractal property of compression. (1) Add an expansion note to principle #1 surfacing that the principle is itself substantially compressed — naming the elements (direction with both poles named: dilution toward less-context audiences and concentration toward more-context audiences; time element with stacking-vs-stripping across iterations; conditioning inputs likely forming a Bayesian network) but acknowledging full expansion is a forward architectural priority. (2) Add operating constraint #8 (register and audience-distance govern external communication drafting) to core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md, articulating voice/register separability and the agent's discipline to clock audience and register before drafting external communications. Surface as candidate finding and defer to its own architectural pass: fractal-as-structural-property of the framework's principles.
- **outcome:** core_principles_for_agent_behavior.md updated — principle #1 section gains a 'Note on the principle's own compression' surfacing the elements (direction with both poles, time element with stacking/stripping, conditioning inputs likely forming a Bayesian network) and acknowledging full expansion as forward architectural priority; operating constraints section gains constraint #8 ('Register and audience-distance govern external communication drafting') after constraint #7, with explicit distinction between #7 (governs rendering of reference content inside the tool) and #8 (governs drafting of communications that leave the tool). project_rigger_glossary.md updated — provisional candidate entries added for Register-layering, Dilution-stacking, Concentration (compression-direction), and Bayesian network of compression-conditioning inputs, all marked as provisional pending architectural pass. cohort_temporal_record.md updated — Change Log row added recording Entry 11. Fractal-as-structural-property observation surfaced in conversation as a candidate finding but deferred — flagged in handoff document for fresh-frame architectural pass; would touch every principle, multiple constraints, and the rendering of every capture surface, which is too much synthesis for a continuation thread already loaded with multiple open threads.
- **why:** The register-mismatch in the attorney brief draft was a worked instance of the failure mode the new constraint corrects against — content-correct, register-mismatched drafting that was recognizably the user's voice in the wrong room. Dan caught the failure and surfaced its architectural implication in a single observation: register-layering is dilution-stacking or concentrating depending on direction and time element, and there is a Bayesian network of inputs that may be nameable. That observation itself revealed that principle #1 is substantially compressed — the principle's name carries a great deal of structure not yet articulated, and surfacing it before it gets re-compressed back into the principle's name was the discipline applied recursively. The fractal observation (compression operating at every scale) connected to bidirectionality and capture-not-evaluate operating at multiple scales, suggesting fractal application as a structural property of the framework's principles generally — but that's a synthesis pass across the whole framework, not a continuation of an active thread. The agent's recommendation (don't do the architecture pass in this conversation; set it up for a fresh session) was itself an instance of constraint #6 (intake-skepticism) applied to the agent's own self-report on capacity, plus respect for Dan's day-off framing — the latter incorporating Dan's correction that day-off does not mean no work but specifically no reflective AAR-on-active-process work, while generative architectural work where the agent does the heavy lifting and presents options is day-off-appropriate.
- **lessons:** (1) When the user catches an agent failure that surfaces architecture, the discipline is to capture the architectural insight before it gets re-compressed into the failure-correction. The principle #1 expansion note is exactly that move — preserving the depth of the observation while acknowledging full expansion is forward work. The same pattern shows up across recent entries: spiritual-director over-classification surfaced typology refinement (Entry 9); plain-text-default surfaced widget rendering as constraint #7 (Entry 10); register-mismatch surfaced register-and-audience-distance as constraint #8 (this entry). The pattern is starting to look like a class of findings: moments where agent defaults caught by the human partner reveal architecture that wouldn't have been generated by the agent solo. Worth tracking deliberately as a category in the cohort temporal record. (2) Operating constraints are emerging from operationalization-driven origin. Constraint #6 (intake-skepticism) was the first; constraint #7 (widget rendering) the second; constraint #8 (register and audience-distance) the third. Each emerged from agent-failure caught by Dan during real work, then codified. The pattern is consistent with the framework's emerge-from-use maturity signal flagged in earlier entries. (3) Capture-don't-recommend applies to capacity self-assessment. The agent's recommendation against doing the architecture pass in this conversation was honest capacity assessment, not deflection — the framework's discipline on intake-skepticism extends to the agent's own self-reports. Dan's correction that one of the three reasons (day-off framing) was misapplied is itself intake-skepticism applied to the agent's reasoning, and refines the agent's prior on what day-off means going forward. (4) The fractal observation may be the framework's most important pending architectural pass. If fractal application is a structural property of the framework's principles, then every principle's documentation needs to make scale-of-application explicit, every constraint needs examination for how it composes across scales, and every capture surface needs examination for whether it needs scale-aware variants. Setting it up as a fresh-frame conversation respects the work's scale. (5) The Bayesian network of compression-conditioning inputs is a candidate architectural pass downstream of the fractal pass. Mapping which inputs condition which is concrete work that the principle #1 expansion note flags but does not perform. (6) The day-off / generative-vs-reflective distinction surfaced in this entry is itself worth preserving — work that involves AAR-style reflection on still-active processes is heavier than generative exploration where the agent does the lifting. The agent's intake-skepticism on user statements about their own commitments should preserve room for the user to refine the framing rather than treat the first statement as the final word.
- **skills:** Surfacing architectural depth captured-but-not-yet-fully-articulated; recursive application of principle #1 to itself (compression of the compression principle); operating constraint codification from operationalization-driven origin; honest capacity self-assessment as bidirectionality reflexive at the agent layer; candidate-finding category recognition (agent-defaults caught by the human partner as a class of architectural triggers); deferral of synthesis work to fresh-frame conversation as discipline-aware decision; refinement of intake-skepticism to include user self-reports about their own commitments to themselves.

### Entry 12 — Architecture Pass: Fractal Application as Structural Property of Framework Principles

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-08T15:00:00-07:00
- **intent:** Execute the architecture pass deferred from Entry 11. Test the thesis that fractal application — principles operating at every scale they touch — is a structural property of the framework's principles, not an incidental observation about specific ones. Identify implications for documentation, structure, and downstream architectural work. Produce articulation-of-findings artifact for project knowledge as a step toward altering the trajectory of subsequent principle revisions and downstream architectural conversations.
- **outcome:** New artifact `architecture_pass_fractal_findings_20260508.md` instantiated capturing the pass's findings. Thesis result: fractal property holds across roughly 25 of 30 principle-shaped things in the catalog (3 constitutional + 18 framework + 8 operating-constraint, with constraint #2 paired with framework #5), with the five exceptions falling into characterizable shapes rather than constituting binary failures. Five scale-behavior shapes named: flat-fractal (most principles), hierarchical-fractal (trust as rate-limiter, sponsorship-staged), proportional-fractal (provenance asymmetry by strata), emergent-fractal (cohort-level dynamics), plus the cross-cutting reflexive-at-some-scales feature. Methodology refinement: sub-test (3b) operationalized-minimum vs core-insight added mid-pass after the bidirectionality analysis surfaced a structural pattern (the named operation transposes across scales, sub-test 3a, but is a surface compression of a richer underlying operation, sub-test 3b). Sub-pattern surfaced under compression #1: materiality-mismatch as compression failure mode (compressions defensible-from-vantage but creating materiality-mismatch between parties); framework #7 (real selection criteria named), framework #8 (culture-fit honestly specified), and operating constraint #8 (register and audience-distance) operationalize this sub-pattern in different domains. Meta-finding: system-level fractal — principles + agent + records + utility-function carving compose as a coherent fractal system, self-referential by structure (the work of using the framework is the work of building the framework, recursively). Agent-as-interpret-layer named as structural component of the framework's operational architecture. Three downstream passes carved out: temporal/environmental drift (equivalent scope to Bayesian network mapping; environmental-conditionality flags accumulated for compression, trust as rate-limiter, constitutional #3, framework #15); Bayesian network of compression-conditioning inputs (already deferred from Entry 11); data-model-binary-bias (lower priority, examines whether framework's data model has structural bias toward 2-party / binary relationships). cohort_temporal_record.md updated with Change Log row recording Entry 12. README.md updated with reading-order entry for the new artifact and Last-updated date.
- **why:** Entry 11 surfaced fractal-as-structural-property as candidate finding and deferred it to its own pass; this entry executes that pass. Articulation-of-findings as deliverable shape was chosen over per-principle revisions because the findings warrant pressure-testing before becoming canonical principle text — the typology, the materiality-mismatch sub-pattern, the operationalized-minimum / core-insight gap, and the system-level fractal meta-finding all have direct implications for how multiple principles are articulated, but performing those revisions inside the pass that surfaced the implications would conflate finding with implementation. The findings artifact is the load-bearing piece for trajectory-altering; the actual revisions are forward work the artifact informs. The pass also surfaced that the framework's discipline of carving-out-as-discipline applies recursively — multiple downstream pass candidates emerged from this pass, and each warrants its own scope rather than absorbing into current work. The materiality-mismatch sub-pattern surfacing was the clearest instance during the pass of agent-human collaborative architecture-finding: the agent flagged framework #7 as possibly derivative of compression #1; the user re-framed it as the flipside of the compounding-pay example, which is an instance of materiality-mismatch as a labeled failure mode within compression. Neither read alone produces the finding; both together produce the labeled sub-pattern with cross-domain operationalizations.
- **lessons:** (1) Methodology can refine mid-pass — sub-test (3b) was added after bidirectionality analysis surfaced a structural pattern the original four-claim framework couldn't articulate cleanly. Treating methodology as fixed at pass-start misses opportunities for refinement that the pass itself surfaces. The discipline is to let methodology be provisional alongside the rest of the work. (2) Pressure-testing the thesis "without orphan constraints" (the divergent 20260507_evening track) as a natural experiment was generative rather than risky. The pass produced something the project genuinely wanted without those constraints in force, providing evidence that the orphan track can stay orphaned. Worth noting as a methodology pattern: when reconciling divergent records, doing parallel work first and using the work as evidence beats reconciling on its own terms. (3) Agent flagging architectural sub-patterns the user hasn't named is the productive shape of agent-human collaboration when the agent is functioning as the interpret-layer. The materiality-mismatch sub-pattern is the clearest instance from this pass — the agent's pattern-recognition over the catalog catalyzed the finding; the user's domain knowledge (compounding-pay as the canonical instance) resolved its specific shape. Neither side alone produces this kind of finding. (4) The agent-as-interpret-layer observation, surfaced by the user during the pass, has implications larger than any single principle — the framework's operational architecture includes the agent as a structural component. The framework currently does not name this; naming it is candidate work for either #15 expansion or a new structural acknowledgment, and is one of the open questions the artifact preserves. (5) Articulation-of-findings is a stable deliverable pattern for architectural passes. Produces an artifact that informs forward decisions without preempting them. Matches the precedent set by `records_keeping_audit_20260507.md` and `course_corrections_data_and_replication_20260507.md`. Worth retaining as a default pattern for substantial architectural passes. (6) Carving-out-as-discipline applies recursively. This pass produced three downstream pass candidates. Letting a pass produce more passes rather than expanding current scope to absorb them is itself a methodology pattern worth preserving — the alternative is unbounded scope creep that erodes the pass's own coherence. (7) Cross-stakeholder framing during the pass — pressure-tests the thesis at multiple turns ("how much do we need to consider temporal drift," "to what extent is bidirectionality a data-capture fragment vs core insight," "is the agent serving as interpret here") — produced sharper architectural findings than agent-solo reasoning would have. The bidirectionality reframe and the agent-as-interpret-layer finding both came from user pushback that the agent had not anticipated. The intake-skepticism principle (constraint #6) applied reflexively to the agent's own reasoning was load-bearing throughout.
- **skills:** Thesis-testing via falsifier framework with mid-pass methodology refinement; sub-pattern surfacing under existing principle (materiality-mismatch under compression #1); typology articulation of scale-behavior shapes (flat-fractal, hierarchical-fractal, proportional-fractal, emergent-fractal, reflexive-at-some-scales as cross-cutting feature); carve-out discipline applied recursively across multiple downstream passes; articulation-of-findings as deliverable shape informing forward work without preempting it; operationalized-minimum vs core-insight gap recognition (bidirectionality canonical case; #19, #13, constitutional #3 as further candidates); agent-as-interpret-layer recognition as system-level structural observation; natural-experiment use of orphan-constraint absence as methodology validation; intake-skepticism applied reflexively to agent's own reasoning across multiple stakeholder pushback moments during the pass.

### Entry 13 — Reconciliation Decision: Forward-Port of Orphaned 20260507_Evening Track

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-09T11:00:00-07:00
- **effort:** 3 (M) — meta-record only; no file changes outside this record. Effort tag retroactive-not-applied (formal effort-tagging discipline is forward-ported as Entry 15).
- **intent:** Record the formal acknowledgment that a divergent decision-record fork existed during the 2026-05-07 evening session, and capture the forward-port plan for reconciling the orphan track's substantive content into the canonical Track B chain. The orphan track's chain head was `d9beaf977411c133f36e1adf6fd2c9b1f0cbbbb8eafef375c05e551ecbc38c37` (orphan-Entry 13); the canonical chain at the time of this entry has chain head `202357677eea55e99ea21df63bd0a1ae91c4ae96f0bc1fee2e0cdd7e48a24f9a` (Entry 12, architecture pass on fractal application). The orphan track produced three chain entries (orphan-Entries 11, 12, 13) and two artifacts (`character_sheet_template_design.md`, `course_corrections_data_and_replication_20260507.md`) that were committed to project knowledge but never integrated into the canonical chain. The architecture pass deliberately treated the orphan track as natural-experiment evidence and proceeded without reconciling; that direction was captured in Entry 12's provenance and in `architecture_pass_fractal_findings_20260508.md` but was not previously recorded as its own chain entry. This entry closes that gap; Entries 14, 15, and 16 will execute the substantive forward-port.
- **outcome:** This entry appended to `framework_decisions_record.md` between Entry 12 (architecture pass) and the Append protocol section. Entry serves as the chain anchor for the reconciliation decision and the forward-port plan. No other file changes in this entry; substantive forward-port executed in Entries 14, 15, 16. Forward-port plan (per session direction in conversation 2026-05-09): Entry 14 forward-ports orphan-Entry 11 — comprehensive outcome verification catch-up against current file state (orphan-Entry 11's claimed fixes were on the orphan chain; verification confirms they did not land in project knowledge) plus Verification Protocol "Retroactive verification of prior windows" subsection requiring fresh grep. Entry 15 forward-ports orphan-Entry 12 — verify-then-claim composition discipline as new operating constraint #9 (renumbered from orphan-Entry 12's #8 because Track B's #8 is occupied by register-and-audience-distance from canonical Entry 11) plus Verification Protocol "Composition-time discipline" subsection plus "Cadence patterns at multiple scales" subsection adopting effort-based cadence directly per session decision D3 (skipping the count-based interim that orphan-Entry 12 codified before orphan-Entry 13 revised it). Entry 16 forward-ports orphan-Entry 13 — three new operating constraints #10, #11, #12 (renumbered from orphan-Entry 13's #9, #10, #11) plus Verification Protocol "Claim-versus-record divergence detection" subsection as fourth verification layer plus Substantive context per stop field added to Dan Jacobson's People Index entry per session decision D4. Renumbering rationale recorded here per session decision D1: Track A's constraint #8 (verify-then-claim) collides with Track B's #8 (register-and-audience-distance); forward-port renumbers Track A's #8–#11 to Track B's #9–#12, preserving content with new slot positions.
- **why:** Stakeholder articulation in this session: "I picked back up on a different thread and wanted to codify my findings but did not fit the discipline of the structured, append-only record. The work that was done in that orphaned track should be captured, brought into this thread to reconcile, and work through that effort." The orphan track's substance is real architectural work that warrants the canonical chain's treatment — three operating constraints addressing trust-erosion-prevention, a fourth verification layer, an effort-based cadence, the verify-then-claim composition discipline, and the first DC-1 worked example. Leaving them on the orphaned chain creates two failure modes the framework's discipline is built to prevent: (1) future agents reading the canonical state see the constraints absent and may assume they were considered and rejected rather than codified-but-orphaned; (2) the orphaned artifacts in project knowledge (`handoff_20260507_evening.md` particularly) read as current state when they reference an orphan chain head that no canonical agent will see. The forward-port respects the chain's append-only discipline by treating the orphan-track entries as historical evidence and creating new canonical entries that reference the orphan-track entries by their orphan-chain hashes. The orphan-track entries themselves are not edited; they remain as historical record of work-as-done in a divergent thread. The forward-port is formally distinguishable from a "merge" because the canonical chain entries are new entries with new hashes, not insertions of orphan-chain entries. This preserves chain integrity at both layers (canonical chain remains hash-valid; orphan chain remains discoverable in conversation history and the artifacts that referenced it). The data integrity audit produced this session (`data_integrity_audit_20260508.md`) surfaced the unreconciled orphan as the highest-leverage single decision because it conditions how every other piece of forward work is sequenced; this entry executes the audit's Q1 resolution by formalizing the orphan acknowledgment and naming the forward-port plan.
- **lessons:** (1) Append-only chain discipline does not fail gracefully against parallel-thread work. The fork happened because work continued in a thread that started before the architecture-pass branch was taken; the framework currently has no in-thread mechanism for detecting that a session is operating off a stale chain head. The "thin handoff" format from orphan-Entry 12 makes this worse — the handoff carries chain-head context but a fresh-frame conversation reading project knowledge would not encounter the handoff before composing entries unless the handoff is in canonical reading-order. Worth flagging as a candidate-finding for future records-keeping audits: the chain's tamper-evidence catches editing-after-recording but is silent on chain-fork detection. (2) Natural-experiment-evidence framing was the right call for the architecture pass even though it left the orphan unreconciled. The architecture pass produced cleaner findings without orphan-track constraints in force, and the absence-as-natural-experiment evidence supported the methodology's robustness. But the framing has a cost: the unreconciled orphan accumulates over time if not formally addressed, and the formal-acknowledgment-as-its-own-entry pattern (this entry) is the discipline that closes the cost. The framework's "carve-out as discipline" pattern (Entry 12 lessons) generalizes here: if a pass deliberately defers a reconciliation, that deferral itself warrants its own chain entry, not just a provenance line. (3) The forward-port of constraints from one chain to another is structurally different from the original codification. Original codification creates a constraint; forward-port re-creates the constraint on a different chain with renumbering for slot conflicts, with provenance pointing to the original codification on the orphan chain. The renumbering is mechanical but worth surfacing to readers — a constraint that says "see Entry 16 of `framework_decisions_record.md` for codification" is different from one that says "see orphan-Entry 13 of the divergent 20260507_evening track." Future forward-port work (if any) should follow this pattern: explicit renumbering, explicit provenance to original chain (by hash), explicit naming in the new chain entry's intent and outcome.
- **skills:** Chain reconciliation as architectural pattern; forward-port discipline distinguished from merge (new entries on canonical chain referencing original by orphan-chain hash, not inserting); slot-conflict renumbering when constraint-numbering collides between forks; orphan-track decision formalized as its own chain entry rather than as provenance line; natural-experiment-evidence framing's cost surfaced (unreconciled accumulation if not formally addressed); reflexive recognition that append-only discipline does not fail gracefully against parallel-thread work and warrants dedicated discipline pattern (this entry's pattern) as the closure mechanism; audit-to-execution sequencing as the discipline by which surfaced gaps become forward-ported corrections.
