CHAIN AUDIT DIMENSION MOVE — fifth canonical instance per Entry 25 first, Entry 29 second, Entry 30 third (three observations), Entry 31 fourth (one observation) baselines. Two audit observations recorded: (1) Thread 2 close handoff `handoff_20260511T030000Z.md` Forward-tags-for-Thread-3 section and the Thread 3 opener composed from it both labeled the chain-audit cross-reference target as "operating constraint #11 (Compare incoming claims to prior records before integrating them)" when canonical Entry 16 forward-port (spine line 247: "renumbered from orphan-Entry 13's #9, #10, #11 to canonical #10, #11, #12 because Track B's #8 and #9 are now occupied by register-and-audience-distance from canonical Entry 11 and verify-then-claim from Entry 15") placed "Compare incoming claims to prior records before integrating them" at canonical constraint #10 with canonical constraint #11 being "When records do not take a position, say so before continuing"; the off-by-one precision overclaim in the handoff's recommendation text was caught at Entry 32 composition time when the verification of constraint cross-references during glossary entry composition surfaced the discrepancy between the handoff's #11 reference and the file's internal cross-references (lines 67, 69, 71 of `core_principles_for_agent_behavior_mod1.md` are mutually consistent on the #10/#11/#12 numbering for the trust-erosion-prevention bundle constraints); corrected in the Entry 32 `Chain audit dimension move` glossary entry which cross-references the canonical operating constraint #10. (2) Entry 31's R2 scope precision on mod-N retention horizon application: R2's catch-up scope (cohort_mod1; glossary_mod1 through _mod4) was defined before Entry 31 produced glossary_mod7 and therefore did not include mod5 even though mod5 became mod(N-2) at mod7's production; this left three glossary mods inline at Entry 31 close (mod5, mod6, mod7) rather than the most-recent-two-mods steady state of two; Entry 32 remediates by enumerating mod5 alongside the natural-demotion mod6 (mod6 becomes mod(N-2) at Entry 32's mod8 production) in this entry's apply-checklist, restoring the steady state to mod7 + mod8 inline; forward-discipline refinement implicit in the remediation: rolling-demotion scope should be re-evaluated at the moment a new mod is produced within an entry rather than only at the moment R-style scoping is performed against pre-entry state. The narrative-claim/apply-checklist gap is the substance of both audit observations; the remedy for observation (1) is the corrected cross-reference in the Entry 32 glossary entry; the remedy for observation (2) is Entry 32's apply-checklist enumeration of mod5 + mod6 with the verify script's ABSENT_FILES set covering both. The codification of the chain audit dimension move at glossary level — including the enumeration of all five canonical instances with their observation counts — is itself the structural response to the discipline's five-canonical-instance recurrence exceeding the framework's ~3-instance recurrence threshold by comfortable margin; promotion-to-glossary was deferred at Entries 25, 29, 30, and 31 to avoid scope-creep on already-bundled entries per the diagnose-before-codify principle's mechanism-agnostic-navigation exemption.
- **why:** Four drivers for this entry. (1) R3 (README restructuring with new `README_changelog.md` sidecar) is the natural continuation of the Thread 2 carve-out per Entry 31's close handoff (R3 + R8 carved to Thread 3). The README had accumulated multi-thousand-character prose in the Last updated line and the Cross-session handshake protocol intro paragraph, plus codification codas on each of the ten protocol components — content that was per-entry historical narrative rather than current-state reference. R3 strips this content from the README (preserving current-state reference and protocol component current-discipline statements) and accumulates it in a reverse-chronological sidecar artifact (`README_changelog.md`) where it can grow without bloating the entry-point document. The R3 split shape was sketched in Entry 31's close handoff and confirmed at Thread 3 opener; the sketch corresponds to the operationalization in this entry. (2) Two glossary codifications were ripe at recurrence threshold and surfaced at Entry 31's close handoff as carry-forwards. `Chain audit dimension move` reached four canonical instances at Entry 31's fourth-instance observation on Entry 30's apply-checklist gap; the discipline's promotion-to-glossary was explicitly deferred at Entries 25, 29, 30, and 31 to avoid scope-creep on already-bundled entries per the diagnose-before-codify principle's mechanism-agnostic-navigation exemption — Entry 32's fifth-instance pressure-test moves the discipline past the ~3-instance recurrence threshold by comfortable margin, and the discipline's structural shape is now well-understood enough to codify cleanly. `Chain-entry-text-as-separate-artifact pattern` reached three canonical instances at Entry 31 (Entry 30 architecture-pass session; Entry 30 Thread 1; Entry 31 Thread 2) — exactly at the ~3-instance recurrence threshold. Bundling both codifications with R3 keeps Thread 3's effort accumulator at L=5 per Entry 23's bundling discipline since they share one composition pass-shape (codification at glossary level using existing mod-N versioning discipline). (3) The most-recent-two-mods rolling demotion at mod-8 production is the natural per-entry application of the `Mod-N retention horizon` extension to `Mod-N versioning` codified in Entry 30. When mod8 is produced, mod6 becomes mod(N-2) and is archive-eligible. The reflexive application of the `Apply-checklist completeness` discipline (Entry 31 codification) at Entry 32 composition time produced the chain audit observation on Entry 31's R2 scope precision: R2's catch-up scope was defined before mod7 was produced and did not include mod5; with mod7 produced, mod5 became mod(N-2) at mod7's production and should have been included in R2's catch-up scope; the gap left three glossary mods inline at Entry 31 close. Entry 32 remediates by enumerating mod5 alongside the natural-demotion mod6, restoring the most-recent-two-mods steady state at mod7 + mod8 inline. (4) The chain audit dimension move's fifth canonical instance with two observations — one on the Thread 2 close handoff off-by-one on operating-constraint numbering, one on Entry 31's R2 scope precision — pressure-tests the discipline at the moment of its codification, validating that the discipline's mechanism (record observations as part of the current entry without rewriting prior entries) handles the two-observation case cleanly. The handoff off-by-one observation also instantiates the meta-pattern of composition-time-cross-reference-verification catching precision overclaims about the framework's own internal numbering — a small but structurally significant pressure-test of mechanical-verification-of-claims-catches-precision-overclaims (principle #23) applied to the framework's own intra-document references.
- **lessons:** (1) Reflexive composition-time application of the `Apply-checklist completeness` discipline (Entry 31 codification) caught the Entry 31 R2 scope precision gap during Entry 32 composition — when scoping the most-recent-two-mods rolling demotion at Entry 32's mod8 production, the question "which mods should the apply-checklist enumerate?" surfaced that mod5 was already overdue (became mod(N-2) at Entry 31's mod7 production) and Entry 32's apply-checklist needed to include both mod5 (catch-up) and mod6 (natural demotion). This is first-instance pressure-test of the Entry 31 codification doing its intended work at composition time of a subsequent entry: the discipline catches gaps that would otherwise propagate. Second-instance pressure-test at the next archive-operation-bearing entry will further confirm. (2) Composition-time cross-reference verification when codifying disciplines that reference other framework components is load-bearing. When the Entry 32 `Chain audit dimension move` glossary entry was being composed, the handoff's recommendation text ("cross-reference to operating constraint #11") was checked against the canonical constraint numbering in `core_principles_for_agent_behavior_mod1.md` (lines 67, 69, 71) and the canonical Entry 16 forward-port narrative (spine line 247) — the cross-check surfaced the off-by-one precision overclaim, which was corrected in the Entry 32 glossary entry. Without composition-time cross-reference verification, the off-by-one would have propagated from the handoff to the glossary entry to all subsequent references. This is a worked example of mechanical-verification-of-claims-catches-precision-overclaims (principle #23) applied to the framework's own intra-document references: when a recommendation in a handoff references a numbered item in another document, verify the number against the cited document at composition time. Carry forward as candidate small framework principle at one-instance pressure-test depth (this thread's application is the first explicit instance; second instance at next codification-referencing-other-numbered-items opportunity will refine or confirm). (3) The R3 split shape (README current-state + README_changelog historical-narrative) validates the broader pattern of pulling per-entry accumulation out of static-named entry-point documents into reverse-chronological sidecar artifacts. The pattern generalizes: any framework artifact whose per-entry historical narrative threatens to bloat its current-state reference can be split into static-named-entry-point + reverse-chronological-changelog-sidecar without restructuring the document's substantive content. Candidate generalization at one-instance pressure-test depth (the R3 application is the first explicit instance); second instance at next static-named-bloat-pressure opportunity will refine. (4) Codification at recurrence threshold versus codification at first instance is a discipline distinction that the Entry 32 bundle pressure-tests at scale. Both glossary codifications (chain audit dimension move at five instances; chain-entry-text pattern at three instances) waited well past the ~3-instance threshold before codifying — the chain audit dimension move particularly so, with explicit deferral notes in Entries 25, 29, 30, and 31. The deferral preserved scope discipline (each instance was bundled with other work; codification was scope-additive); the eventual codification at threshold benefits from the multi-instance pressure-test producing a richer glossary entry than first-instance codification would have. This is the diagnose-before-codify principle's mechanism-agnostic exemption working as designed: defer mechanism-claiming codification until the mechanism is well-understood; mechanism-agnostic navigation moves (recording the observation; enumerating the canonical instances) remain available throughout the deferral. (5) The chain audit dimension move's fifth canonical instance with two observations — one structural-precision (off-by-one on constraint numbering) and one scope-precision (R2 mod5 catch-up gap) — instantiates the discipline's coverage of both subtype-of-precision-overclaim categories: claims about framework-internal facts (which constraint number? which entry codified what?) and claims about framework-internal-discipline scope (what does the most-recent-two-mods discipline apply to? when does rolling demotion fire?). The glossary entry's enumeration explicitly names both categories with their canonical examples. (6) Pass-shape lexicon advance 10 → 11. Eighth distinct category: 'README restructuring with sidecar changelog instantiation + bundled glossary codifications at recurrence threshold + most-recent-two-mods rolling demotion with catch-up + chain audit dimension move.' Distinguished from Entry 31's seventh category by being README-restructure-led with codification as the wing (vs. Entry 31's operationalization-led with codification as the wing) and by combining a structural restructure (R3) with bundled glossary-level codifications (vs. Entry 31's operating-constraint-scope-extension at higher principle tier). (7) Reflexive composition-time application of framework principle #23 (mechanical-verification-of-claims-catches-precision-overclaims) at multiple points: composition-time SHA-256 capture on mod5+mod6 from canonical /mnt/project/ state (74,600 bytes / `fa46a183097a35ac5fb02d4ffc1dea23e705d9f7ca165f3eaeba457d047b837b` and 85,906 bytes / `d257f4f6b37a438b6342064bf727362e5666d0d0e8953f2b16618ff5c3129b95` respectively) rather than carried-forward-from-prior-entry; composition-time cross-reference verification of operating constraint numbering against canonical file lines 67/69/71 and Entry 16 forward-port narrative (spine line 247); composition-time alphabetic-insertion-order verification of two new glossary entries (Case study line 33 → Chain audit dimension move line 35 → Chain-entry-text-as-separate-artifact pattern line 37 → Character sheet line 39 → CM-callout surface line 41); PRIOR_HASHES capture verified that the README.md and _archive_manifest.md baselines match Entry 31's EXPECTED canonical values (no transmission drift between Entry 31 close and Entry 32 open per verify_entry_31.py 68/68 PASS at Thread 3 opener); reflexive verify against working state before file presentation will confirm 100% PASS or surface the residual gap. Fourteenth-instance recurrence at composition time across Entries 21–32. (8) The codification of `Chain audit dimension move` as a named glossary discipline closes a meta-loop: the discipline that records observations on prior chain entries is itself codified as part of a chain entry that records observations on prior chain entries (Entry 32's own fifth-instance observations). The recursive structure is a pressure-test of the discipline's coherence — the discipline survives self-application cleanly.

### Entry 33 — Cross-Session Handshake Protocol Restructured from Ten Peer-Level Components into Five Top-Level Groups with Sub-Parts (Cold-Start Absorption Optimization) + Replacement-Always Modification Discipline Codified as Sub-Part 1(d) (Producer Composes Full Replacement; Append-Style Outputs Deprecated as Non-Canonical) + Verify-Tools External-Storage Tier Elevated from Parenthetical to Explicit Sub-Part 3(a) (Three-Instance Pressure-Test) + Cross-Reference Strategy Codified in Outcome (Update-Live-Spec; Leave-Historical-Records-As-Composed; No Per-Reference Chain-Audit Notes)

- **timestamp:** 2026-05-11T17:00:00-07:00
- **effort:** 5 (L) — Thread 5 closing chain entry executing the Thread 4 in-conversation diagnosis of two cross-session handshake protocol friction surfaces (cold-start re-derivation cost from ten heterogeneous peer-level components failing to compress for new sessions; append-vs-replace inconsistency dumping merge work on the stakeholder) plus the codification-ripe verify-tools tier elevation carried forward from Thread 2 architectural call. Three bundled sub-codifications per Entry 23's bundling discipline, sharing one composition pass-shape (protocol-component restructure with bundled sub-discipline codifications and cross-reference strategy): (A) reorganization of the protocol's ten peer-level components into five top-level groups with sub-parts following session-to-session lifecycle ordering (file conventions → handoff form and retention → post-commit verification → opener dependency-ordering → navigation-under-failure); (B) replacement-always modification discipline codified as sub-part 1(d) under the file-conventions group, naming the producer-composes-full-replacement output shape and explicitly deprecating append-style outputs as non-canonical for modifications to static-named and mod-N versioned files; (C) verify-tools external-storage tier elevated from parenthetical in former-component-#3 to explicit sub-part 3(a) under the post-commit-verification group, completing the three-instance pressure-test (Thread 2 architectural call + Entry 31 `verify_entry_31.py` landing pattern + Entry 32 reflexive application). Five produced/modified files: new timestamped spine `framework_decisions_record_20260512T000000Z.md`; modified `README.md` (handshake-protocol section replaced; pointer-block updates; last-updated stamp advanced); modified `README_changelog.md` (Entry 33 section prepended per reverse-chronological discipline); new verification routine `verify_entry_33.py` (kept external to canonical project knowledge per Thread 2 architectural cleanup; pattern follows `verify_entry_32.py`); new handoff `handoff_20260512T000000Z.md`. Bundle width is rhetorical not effort-additive per Entry 23's bundling discipline — three sub-codifications share one composition pass-shape and produce coordinated content across 5 artifacts. Pass-shape: protocol-component restructure with bundled sub-discipline codifications and cross-reference strategy (ninth distinct pass-shape category in the framework's pass-shape lexicon under Entry 25's codified counting convention; first canonical instance of this category). Pass-shape lexicon count advances 11 → 12 distinct categories. Per the per-thread reset semantic codified in Entry 29 (Co-equal session limiters), Thread 5's effort accumulator runs 0 → 5/15 (L=5 for this entry; all three bundle elements absorbed per the bundling discipline); Thread 5's handoff-instance counter runs 0 → 1, hitting the N=1 threshold and triggering session closure after Entry 33's close handoff. Carry-forward deferred items per the Thread 4 scoping handoff's preserved-deferred set carve to Thread 6.
- **intent:** Operationalize the Thread 4 in-conversation diagnosis of two friction surfaces in the cross-session handshake protocol (currently codified as ten components in `README.md` lines 69–91, post-Entry-32 R3): (1) the cold-start re-derivation problem — ten heterogeneous peer-level components don't compress into something a new session can absorb cold, so the protocol fails at its own job (instead of saving re-derivation cost, it imposes it); (2) the append-vs-replace inconsistency — the protocol doesn't enforce that producing-session outputs are full-file replacements, so append-style outputs (which in this operating environment mean stakeholder-side merge work) remain permissible by default, putting cost on the wrong actor. Execute three coordinated changes in one bundled entry. (A) **Reorganize the ten components into five top-level groups with sub-parts.** Lifecycle-ordered: (1) File conventions [was #2 + #8 + #9 + new sub-part for B], (2) Handoff form and retention [was #1 + #10], (3) Post-commit verification [was #3 + #5 + #6, with C: verify-tools tier elevation], (4) Opener dependency-ordering [was #4], (5) Navigation-under-failure [was #7]. Sub-parts where the discipline has genuine layered structure (file conventions has 4 sub-parts; post-commit verification has 2 sub-parts with the second containing a 5-layer enumeration; handoff form and retention has 2 sub-parts; opener dependency-ordering and navigation-under-failure each stand alone). The reorganization compresses cold-start absorption cost by giving the protocol structurally honest groupings — a new session can scan the five top-level group names and immediately know which group a given operational question falls into, rather than needing to re-derive heterogeneous peers' relationships. (B) **Codify the replacement-always modification discipline as sub-part 1(d) under the file-conventions group.** Discipline: the producing session always composes the full replacement file for any modification to a static-named or mod-N versioned file; append-style outputs ('add this section to X.md'; 'append this row to Y.md') become non-canonical because they mean the stakeholder is doing the merge manually in this operating environment, and composition cost on the producer side is cheaper than migration cost on the stakeholder side (the producer has working state, full context, and reflexive verification surface; the stakeholder has only the patch fragment and must apply it without verification surface). The discipline applies to all canonical-state file modifications — static-named entry-point and sidecar documents (e.g. `README.md`, `README_changelog.md`, `_archive_manifest.md`), static-named canonical artifacts (`framework_principles.md` while lazy at pre-Entry-24 state), and mod-N versioned lateral reference files (the mod-N transition itself is replacement-by-rename, but subsequent edits to a given mod also follow replacement-always). The discipline does not apply to the timestamped spine and the handoff (both produced as new files at each entry per Entry 24's discipline, so there's no append-vs-replace ambiguity for them). Sub-part placement under file conventions rather than as a separate top-level group: the discipline is keyed to file categories (static-named, mod-N versioned) defined in sub-parts 1(a)–1(c); placing it alongside them under one umbrella keeps the relationship visible to a cold-start reader. (C) **Elevate verify-tools external-storage tier from parenthetical to explicit sub-part 3(a) under the post-commit-verification group.** Discipline: per-entry verify scripts live in external storage (private archive repo, `verify_tools/` subdirectory) rather than inline in canonical project knowledge. Three-instance pressure-test depth: Thread 2 architectural call (verify scripts pushed to GitHub private `verify_tools/`); Entry 31 first applied (`verify_entry_31.py` stored externally); Entry 32 reflexively applied (`verify_entry_32.py` produced as external, handoff apply-checklist row 6 explicitly labeled `NEW (external)` with `(self-bootstrapping; not in own manifest)` note). Elevated from inside former-component-#3 parenthetical to explicit sub-part 3(a) status, completing the operating-discipline visibility of where verify lives. Apply hierarchical-principle-orientation tier-ordering per Entries 31–32 pattern: the disciplines being codified (replacement-always; verify-tools tier) belong at protocol-component tier (operating discipline visible at session-to-session handshake), not at glossary tier (terminology lookup) or operating-constraint tier (agent-output constraint). Apply composition-time cross-reference verification per Entry 32 first-instance pressure-test: the reorganization renumbers components, so every reference to `component #N` elsewhere in the corpus must have an explicit strategy declared in this entry's outcome field — the strategy codifies update-canonical for live-spec references and leave-historical for immutable historical records, avoiding 100+ targeted micro-edits to chronological breadcrumbs. Apply reflexive composition-time mechanical-verification-of-claims (framework principle #23): pressure-test instances for the replacement-always codification enumerated at composition time by inspection of prior chain entries' apply-checklists (Entries 30, 31, 32 all REPLACE static-named files with full-file composed outputs implicitly applying the discipline; Entry 33 itself is the first explicit application of replacement-always to README_changelog modification since it ships the Entry 33 section as part of a full-file composed replacement rather than as an append patch instruction). Apply apply-checklist completeness with Layer 6 candidate (Entry 32 miss): present_files bundles every file the stakeholder needs including verify_entry_33.py itself for convenience. Apply state-at-time-t reporting with `[baseline + trend × normalizing factor]` decomposition per Thread 3 close one-instance pressure-test: top-level component count `5 = 10 − 5 (peer-to-group consolidation)`; sub-part count `8 = 0 + 8 (layered-structure exposure)`; sub-sub-part count within 3(b) layered-verification-surface `5 = 0 + 5 (L0/L1/L1.5/L2/L3 layer enumeration)`. Reflexively apply the replacement-always discipline at composition time of this entry itself: README, README_changelog ship as full-file REPLACE rows in the apply-checklist, not as 'add this section' or 'modify these lines' patches. Per the per-thread reset semantic codified in Entry 29, Thread 5 opens at 0/15 effort and 0/1 handoff-instance, advances to 5/15 effort (L=5 with all three bundle elements absorbed per Entry 23's bundling discipline) and 1/1 handoff-instance (at threshold, triggering session closure after Entry 33's close handoff). Carry-forward deferred items per the Thread 4 scoping handoff's preserved-deferred set carve to Thread 6.

Per-reference strategy: (i) **Update** references in live-spec artifacts that point at the protocol as a current specification — `README.md` itself, `README_changelog.md` spec-pointer sections that reference current numbering, the current canonical glossary mod's live cross-refs (none currently — glossary entries reference the *concepts* not the protocol numbering), `framework_principles.md` if it carries any, Entry 33's own forward-references in the new spine. (ii) **Leave-as-historical** references in immutable historical records — all prior chain entry text across spines (each entry's component numbering means 'as numbered at the time of that entry's composition'; rewriting them would corrupt the historical record and break chain-hash integrity since prior entries are byte-identical in the new spine), all retained handoffs (chronological breadcrumbs with the same time-scoped semantic), `_archive_manifest.md` entries referencing archived files' contents. (iii) **No per-reference chain-audit-dimension note attached** — the rule itself lives in this Entry 33 outcome and is the canonical disambiguation surface; per-reference notes would add 100+ targeted micro-edits to historical artifacts for marginal additional clarity over the single canonical rule statement. The rationale: chain-audit-dimension move (Entry 32 codification) is the right surface for observations *about prior entries that were misnumbered at composition time* (off-by-one number; scope-precision gap); this entry's renumbering is *not retroactive correction* but *forward structural change*, so the chain-audit dimension does not apply — the appropriate surface is the entry's outcome documenting the strategy explicitly. Future readers of any prior chain entry's 'per component #N' reference apply: 'as numbered at the time of that entry; per Entry 33's reorganization, see the 5-group structure in the post-Entry-33 README for current numbering and consult the README_changelog Entry 33 section for the mapping.' **REFLEXIVE COMPOSITION-TIME APPLICATION OF REPLACEMENT-ALWAYS (sub-part 1(d) discipline being codified):** README and README_changelog ship in this entry's apply-checklist as full-file REPLACE rows with EXPECTED SHA-256 hashes captured at composition time, not as 'modify these lines' patches. The reflexive application is the first explicit instance of replacement-always being applied to a modification of `README_changelog.md` (Entry 32 instantiated the file as NEW; Entry 33 is the first content-modifying entry, since the changelog grows by prepending per the reverse-chronological discipline). The discipline working in both directions — past-implicit (Entries 30, 31, 32 each shipped REPLACE rows for static-named files, applying the discipline without naming it) and forward-explicit (Entry 33 names the discipline and reflexively applies it) — completes the codification with first-instance explicit pressure-test depth. **PASS-SHAPE LEXICON ADVANCE (11 → 12 distinct categories):** Ninth distinct pass-shape category, first canonical instance: 'protocol-component restructure with bundled sub-discipline codifications and cross-reference strategy.' Distinguished from Entry 32's eighth category (README restructuring with sidecar changelog instantiation + bundled glossary codifications at recurrence threshold + chain audit dimension move) by being protocol-component-restructure-led with discipline-codification as the wing (vs. Entry 32's README-restructure-led with glossary-codifications as the wing) and by codifying disciplines at protocol-component tier rather than glossary tier. **STAKEHOLDER-SIDE ACTIONS PER ENTRY 33 APPLY-CHECKLIST (executed through project-knowledge interface after verify_entry_33.py 100% PASS confirms the produced files landed): None beyond the file drops** — no archive operations, no REMOVE operations, no GitHub pushes. Entry 33's apply-checklist is single-phase: drop 4 files into project knowledge (5th file is verify_entry_33.py, external storage), then verify.
- **why:** Three drivers for this entry. (1) **Cold-start re-derivation cost was structurally undermining the protocol's own purpose.** The cross-session handshake protocol exists to let a new session pick up where the prior session left off without re-reading everything; what a new session needs is the chain head, the open-work surface, and the load-bearing artifacts the most recent work produced. But the protocol itself had grown to ten peer-level components covering heterogeneous concerns (file-naming alongside verification cluster alongside retention alongside navigation-under-failure), and the heterogeneity meant a cold-start session had to re-derive from scratch each time what the optimal protocol pattern is and how to handle it. The protocol failed at its own job: instead of saving re-derivation cost, it imposed it. The Thread 4 in-conversation diagnosis surfaced this failure mode at user-stated friction ('protocols are annoying') and Thread 5 closes the effort with the reorganization. Five top-level groups in session-to-session lifecycle order (producer ships under G1's conventions → G2's handoff form → receiver runs G3's verify → reads via G4's dependency-ordering → G5 if verify persistently fails) compress cold-start absorption cost: a new session can scan the five group names and know immediately which group a given operational question falls under, without re-deriving relationships among heterogeneous peers. (2) **Append-vs-replace inconsistency dumped data-migration work on the stakeholder.** The protocol didn't enforce that producing-session outputs were full-file replacements; append-style outputs ('add this row to X.md') were permissible by default. But in this operating environment append-style outputs mean the stakeholder is doing the merge manually — composing the new content from a Claude-provided patch and integrating it into the existing file through the project-knowledge interface. This is silly when the producing session could just compose the full new file: composition cost on the producer side is cheaper than migration cost on the stakeholder side (the producer has working state, can compose with full context, and can run reflexive verification; the stakeholder has only the patch fragment and must apply it without verification surface). The discipline should make this choice in advance, not punt it. Entry 33 codifies replacement-always as sub-part 1(d) under the file-conventions group, naming the discipline explicitly and deprecating append-style outputs as non-canonical for modifications to static-named and mod-N versioned files. The codification is forward-prescriptive (future modifications follow replacement-always); past instances of implicit application (Entries 30, 31, 32 each shipped REPLACE rows for README and _archive_manifest) are pressure-test depth confirming the discipline is operationally viable at scale. (3) **Verify-tools external-storage tier was three-instance pressure-test ripe and codification-ready.** Thread 2 made the architectural call to push verify scripts to GitHub private `verify_tools/`. Entry 31 first applied the discipline (verify_entry_31.py stored externally). Entry 32 reflexively applied it (verify_entry_32.py produced as external, handoff apply-checklist row 6 explicitly labeled `NEW (external)`). The discipline of where verify lives had visibility at protocol-component tier — but only as a parenthetical inside former-component-#3 ('Per-entry verify scripts live in external storage [...] per the Thread 2 architectural cleanup'). Three-instance pressure-test depth on the discipline as applied warrants elevation from parenthetical to explicit sub-part. Bundled with the verification-cluster reorganization (which collapsed former-#3 + former-#5 + former-#6 into one G3 with sub-parts 3a per-entry-verify-artifact and 3b layered-verification-surface) so the elevation lands as a natural sub-part 3(a) of the reorganized group rather than as a separate codification. Apply hierarchical-principle-orientation per Entries 31–32: the discipline of where verify scripts live is operating-discipline (where the file exists in the storage hierarchy), not glossary terminology and not agent-output operating-constraint, so protocol-component tier is the right surface. (4) **Hierarchical-principle-orientation third-instance pressure-test ripe at Entry 33 even though codification deferred per pressure-test-thin status.** Entry 31 first instance: codification placement at operating-constraint extension (`Apply-checklist completeness` placed at operating-constraint tier because the visibility requirement was at composition-time agent-output-constraint). Entry 32 second instance: codification placement at glossary level (`Chain audit dimension move` placed at glossary tier because the visibility requirement was at composition-time-reference for records-keeping discipline). Entry 33 third instance: codification placement at protocol-component tier (`Replacement-always for modifications` and `Verify-tools external-storage tier` placed at protocol-component tier because the visibility requirement is at session-to-session handshake for protocol disciplines). Three instances now refine the framework: disciplines belong where the visibility requirement places them in the principle hierarchy, *and* the visibility requirement varies by discipline scope (agent-output disciplines warrant operating-constraint tier; records-keeping disciplines warrant glossary tier; protocol/handshake disciplines warrant protocol-component tier; meta-principles warrant constitutional tier). Entry 33 does *not* codify the framework-level hierarchical-principle-orientation discipline at glossary level despite reaching the three-instance recurrence threshold, because the Thread 4 scoping handoff explicitly carry-forwarded it as pressure-test-thin and the opener directed Thread 6+ for codification — deferring respects the diagnose-before-codify principle and avoids scope-creep on an already-bundled Thread 5 closing entry. Reflects in the lessons field as third-instance observation, candidate for Thread 6 codification.
- **lessons:** (1) **Cold-start absorption cost is a real protocol cost, not a stylistic concern.** Ten heterogeneous peer-level components don't compress for a new session even when each individual component is well-written, because the session has to re-derive the relationship among them. Five top-level groups in lifecycle order compress because the session has structural anchors (producer → handoff → verify → reading → failure-handling) that don't require re-derivation. Generalizes: protocol sections grown to ~8+ peer components are candidate for structural reorganization regardless of whether individual components are well-written; the cost is at the relationship-among-components level, not at the per-component-quality level. Carry forward as candidate small framework principle at one-instance pressure-test depth (this entry's reorganization is the first explicit instance); second instance at next protocol-section-grown-large opportunity will refine or confirm. (2) **Replacement-always as a discipline closes the cost-asymmetry between producer and stakeholder.** The producer has working state, full context, and reflexive verification surface; the stakeholder has only the patch fragment and the project-knowledge interface. Composition cost on the producer side is much cheaper than migration cost on the stakeholder side, even though composing a full file feels heavier than emitting a patch. The discipline names this asymmetry and resolves it in the producer's direction by default, putting the right cost on the right actor. Generalizes to other operator-environment asymmetries: when producer-side cost and consumer-side cost differ by order of magnitude for the same outcome, the discipline should make the choice prospectively rather than leaving it implicit. (3) **Composition-time cross-reference verification (Entry 32 first instance; this entry second instance) refines into operational rule with two sub-modes.** The Entry 32 first instance was constraint-numbering off-by-one catch (point-precision overclaim). This entry's second instance is the renumbering-impact scoping for 127 references across 17 files — a different shape (not point-precision but bulk-impact). The two instances together refine: composition-time cross-reference verification has at least two sub-modes — (i) point-precision-overclaim catches when a discipline being codified references another framework component's numbered item (Entry 32 first instance), and (ii) bulk-impact-strategy decisions when a structural change renumbers items that other documents reference (Entry 33 second instance). The two sub-modes share the underlying discipline (verify the number/reference against the cited document at composition time) but differ in the operational response (correct the overclaim vs declare the cross-reference strategy in the outcome). Carry forward as small framework principle at two-instance pressure-test depth (still pressure-test-thin per the scoping handoff's classification); third instance at next cross-reference-impact opportunity will codify. (4) **Cross-reference strategy as outcome-field discipline (rather than per-reference chain-audit notes) scales better than per-reference micro-edits.** Entry 33 has 127 prior `component #N` references across 17 files affected by the renumbering. Per-reference chain-audit-dimension notes would mean 100+ targeted micro-edits to historical artifacts for marginal clarity over a single canonical rule statement. Entry 33 codifies the rule in its outcome field (update live-spec references; leave-as-historical immutable-record references; no per-reference notes); future readers of prior chain entries' 'per component #N' apply 'as numbered at the time of that entry; see Entry 33 for current numbering.' The single canonical rule is the disambiguation surface; the prior references stay byte-identical for chain-hash integrity. Generalizes: structural renumbering changes warrant outcome-field strategy declaration rather than per-reference edits because (a) prior chain entry text is immutable for chain-hash integrity reasons, (b) handoffs and archive manifests are chronological breadcrumbs whose time-scoped semantic is preserved by leaving them as-composed, (c) one canonical rule scales better than N targeted edits. Distinguished from chain-audit-dimension move (Entry 32 codification): chain-audit applies to *retroactive corrections of prior-entry composition-time observations* (off-by-one number; scope-precision gap); outcome-field strategy applies to *forward structural changes that affect prior references' interpretation* without retroactively correcting them. Both surfaces exist; this entry distinguishes when each applies. (5) **Sub-part structure where the discipline has genuine layered structure produces more honest codification than top-level enumeration.** Former-component-#5 (hash-manifest) and former-component-#6 (modification-confirmation) were peer-level components that described Layer 1 and Layer 0 respectively. The layered nature of the discipline (composition-time PRIOR ≠ EXPECTED inequality catches no-write; post-commit hash-manifest catches transmission drift; absence check catches incomplete REMOVE; content-pattern is diagnostic; chain-integrity authenticates the entry) was hidden by the peer-level placement. Refolding former-#5 + former-#6 + the implicit absence check (codified at Entry 31 but not surfaced at component level) into a single sub-part 3(b) 'Layered verification surface' with explicit five-layer enumeration (L0/L1/L1.5/L2/L3) makes the structure visible to a cold-start session in one place. Generalizes: when multiple peer-level protocol components share a generative structure, refolding them into one component with sub-parts is the codification move; the layered structure was always there, the prior enumeration was just hiding it. Candidate generalization at one-instance pressure-test depth (this entry's refolding of #5+#6+absence-check into 3(b) is the first explicit instance); second instance at next refold-opportunity will refine. (6) **Pass-shape lexicon advance 11 → 12. Ninth distinct pass-shape category, first canonical instance: 'protocol-component restructure with bundled sub-discipline codifications and cross-reference strategy.'** Distinguished from Entry 32's eighth category (README restructuring with sidecar changelog instantiation + bundled glossary codifications at recurrence threshold + chain audit dimension move) by being protocol-component-restructure-led with discipline-codification as the wing (vs. Entry 32's README-restructure-led with glossary-codifications as the wing). Distinguished from Entry 31's seventh category (operationalization of prior architecture-pass recommendations + bundled codification at operating-constraint scope extension) by being structural reorganization with codifications at protocol-component tier (vs. Entry 31's operationalization with codifications at operating-constraint tier and glossary tier). (7) **Hierarchical-principle-orientation third-instance pressure-test (Entry 31 first; Entry 32 second; Entry 33 third) — three instances at recurrence threshold, codification deferred to Thread 6 per scoping handoff classification of the discipline as pressure-test-thin.** Entry 31 placed `Apply-checklist completeness` at operating-constraint tier (visibility requirement at composition-time agent-output-constraint). Entry 32 placed `Chain audit dimension move` at glossary tier (visibility requirement at composition-time-reference for records-keeping discipline). Entry 33 places `Replacement-always for modifications` and `Verify-tools external-storage tier` at protocol-component tier (visibility requirement at session-to-session-handshake for protocol-component disciplines). Three instances refine the framework: disciplines belong where the visibility requirement places them in the principle hierarchy, *and* the visibility requirement varies by discipline scope. Codification of the meta-discipline at framework level deferred to Thread 6 per the scoping handoff's pressure-test-thin classification — diagnose-before-codify protects against premature codification when the mechanism is still under refinement. Carry-forward at three-instance pressure-test depth, with the three placements (operating-constraint, glossary, protocol-component) as the canonical example set. (8) **Reflexive composition-time application of framework principle #23 (mechanical-verification-of-claims-catches-precision-overclaims) at multiple points.** Fifteenth-instance recurrence at composition time across Entries 21–33. (a) Replacement-always pressure-test enumeration: prior chain entries' apply-checklists inspected at composition time to confirm Entries 30, 31, 32 each shipped REPLACE rows for static-named files (validating the discipline is operationally viable at scale, not just an aspirational codification). (b) Cross-reference scope grep at composition time: 127 references across 17 files surfaced by `grep -rn 'component #' --include='*.md' --include='*.py'`, informing the cross-reference strategy in the outcome. (c) PRIOR_HASHES capture from canonical /mnt/project/ state confirmed README.md and README_changelog.md baselines match Entry 32's EXPECTED canonical values (no transmission drift between Entry 32 close and Entry 33 open per verify_entry_32.py 43/43 PASS at Thread 5 opener Step 1 after stakeholder applied the two REMOVE rows previously missed in canonical state). (d) Composition-time pass-shape category verification: ninth category framing checked against the existing eight-category lexicon to confirm structural distinctness rather than restatement. Reflexive verify against working state before file presentation will confirm 100% PASS or surface the residual gap.
- **skills:** Cross-session handshake protocol restructured from ten peer-level components to five top-level groups with sub-parts (lifecycle-ordered: file conventions → handoff form and retention → post-commit verification → opener dependency-ordering → navigation-under-failure); replacement-always modification discipline codified as sub-part 1(d) under file-conventions group at first canonical instance (the producer-composes-full-replacement output shape with append-style outputs deprecated as non-canonical for modifications to static-named and mod-N versioned files); verify-tools external-storage tier elevated from parenthetical to explicit sub-part 3(a) under post-commit-verification group at three-instance pressure-test depth (Thread 2 architectural call + Entry 31 verify_entry_31.py landing pattern + Entry 32 reflexive application); five-layer enumeration L0/L1/L1.5/L2/L3 refolded from former-component-#5 + former-component-#6 + Entry 31 implicit absence-check into explicit sub-part 3(b) 'Layered verification surface'; cross-reference strategy codified in this entry's outcome (update live-spec references; leave-as-historical immutable-record references; no per-reference chain-audit notes) covering 127 `component #N` references across 17 files affected by the renumbering; reflexive composition-time application of the replacement-always discipline at Entry 33 itself (README and README_changelog ship as full-file REPLACE rows in apply-checklist with EXPECTED SHA-256 hashes); three bundle elements absorbed at L=5 effort per Entry 23's bundling discipline (reorganization + replacement-always codification + verify-tools tier elevation; all three share one composition pass-shape: protocol-component restructure with bundled sub-discipline codifications and cross-reference strategy); state-at-time-t reporting with `[baseline + trend × normalizing factor]` decomposition: top-level component count `5 = 10 − 5 (peer-to-group consolidation)`; sub-part count `8 = 0 + 8 (layered-structure exposure)`; sub-sub-part count within 3(b) `5 = 0 + 5 (L0/L1/L1.5/L2/L3 layer enumeration)`; hierarchical-principle-orientation third-instance pressure-test (Entry 31 first at operating-constraint tier; Entry 32 second at glossary tier; pressure-test-thin: hierarchical-principle-orientation insight now at three-instance threshold, composition-time cross-reference verification at two-instance, static-named-entry-point + reverse-chronological-changelog-sidecar pattern at one-instance, state-representation-as-vector at one-instance, session opener template now at four-instance recurrence with the diagnosis-derived sub-shape variant flagged in Thread 5 opener provenance note).

- **intent:** Codify two glossary entries at glossary tier per the Thread 5 close handoff's Carry-forward set, bundled per Entry 23's bundling discipline since both codifications share one composition pass-shape (small bundled glossary codifications at glossary tier; advance mod8 → mod9 under Entry 24's mod-N versioning discipline). (A) **Hierarchical-principle-orientation** — codify the meta-discipline naming where a discipline belongs in the framework's principle hierarchy, with the visibility-requirement principle as the operative rule (agent has to remember this while composing → operating-constraint tier; agent looks this up when invoking the move → glossary tier; session opener has to see this → protocol-component tier; foundational to the framework's operation → constitutional or framework-principle tier). Three canonical instances at Entry 33 close meet the framework's ~3-instance recurrence threshold: Entry 31 placed `Apply-checklist completeness` at operating-constraint tier (composition-time agent-output-constraint visibility); Entry 32 placed `Chain audit dimension move` at glossary tier (composition-time-reference visibility); Entry 33 placed `Replacement-always for modifications` and `Verify-tools external-storage tier` at protocol-component tier (session-to-session-handshake visibility). Reflexive self-placement: hierarchical-principle-orientation is itself a records-keeping discipline (it tells composers which tier to use), so by its own rule it belongs at glossary tier — the codification's placement is self-consistent under the rule it codifies, which is structurally honest and a small confirmation that the rule's geometry is internally coherent. Distinguished from `Tapeworm recursion` (the static-name caching mechanism for individual files within an upload batch) by failure mode: tapeworm is a content-fidelity failure on individual static-named files; bulk-upload-scramble is a permutation failure on the upload batch's ordering or completeness irrespective of filename. Both fire at upload time; both have mechanism-agnostic navigation moves under diagnose-before-codify (principle #22) since the underlying causes remain undiagnosed. - **why:** Three drivers for this entry. (1) **Hierarchical-principle-orientation reached three-instance recurrence threshold at Entry 33 close, ripe for codification at Thread 6 open per the Thread 5 close handoff's deferral.** The three canonical instances (Entry 31 operating-constraint tier; Entry 32 glossary tier; Entry 33 protocol-component tier) refine the framework's understanding of how to place new disciplines: the visibility-requirement is the operative criterion (where does the discipline need to be visible during operational use?), and the visibility-requirement varies by discipline scope (agent-output disciplines need operating-memory presence; records-keeping disciplines need composition-time-reference availability; protocol disciplines need session-to-session-handshake visibility; meta-principles need framework-design-time invariance). Without codification, the meta-discipline lives only as deferred-codification observation across three entries' lessons fields, requiring reconstruction each time a new codification's tier-placement decision arises. Codifying at glossary tier surfaces the discipline at composition-time-reference (where the next composer needs it) and is reflexively self-consistent under the rule being codified (records-keeping discipline → glossary tier). Codification deferred from Entry 33 per scope-creep avoidance is now appropriate at Thread 6 open as Thread 6's opening substance, applying the diagnose-before-codify principle's three-instance threshold cleanly. (2) **Bulk-upload-scramble class + folder-batch-granularity discipline both-sides pressure-tested in Thread 2 and codification-ripe at Thread 6.** Both-sides pressure-testing is structurally different from same-direction recurrence-threshold pressure-testing: the contrast operation (bulk-all upload) and the disciplined operation (folder-by-folder upload) were each tested in Thread 2 and confirmed their respective behaviors (bulk scrambles non-deterministically; folder-by-folder lands deterministically). The two-sided pressure-test establishes both the mechanism's existence and the navigation move's effectiveness simultaneously, which is the equivalent of three-instance same-direction pressure-test for codification candidacy. Distinguishing from tapeworm-recursion (Entry 24 codification) is important because both fire at upload time but with diagnostically separable signatures; without the codification at glossary level, future sessions encountering upload anomalies have to re-derive the diagnostic distinction. The discipline is mechanism-agnostic per diagnose-before-codify (principle #22) since the underlying cause of the scramble remains undiagnosed; what's codified is the operational response (upload folder-by-folder), not the mechanism claim. (3) **Bundle elements share one composition pass-shape; bundling discipline (Entry 23) collapses two-discipline codification to S=1 effort.** Both glossary codifications produce coordinated content across the same six artifacts (spine + glossary mod9 + README + README_changelog + verify_entry_34.py + handoff), share the alphabetical-insertion composition shape in the glossary, share the pointer-block-update composition shape in the README, share the section-prepend composition shape in the README_changelog, and share the apply-checklist composition shape in the handoff. Bundle width is rhetorical not effort-additive: two codifications fit in one S=1 entry per the bundling discipline, leaving Thread 6's effort accumulator at 1/15 after Entry 34. The pass-shape (small bundled glossary codifications at glossary tier without chain-audit-move attachment) is the third canonical instance of Entry 25's fifth pass-shape category; pass-shape lexicon count holds at 12 (no new shape introduced). (4) **Mod-N cap concern surfaced in Thread 6 opening conversation, captured as carry-forward not as Entry 34 scope.** Stakeholder signal at Thread 6 open ('we need to cap mod-N at some point') indicates the unbounded-monotonic-growth dimension of mod-N proliferation is a separate concern from the inline-retention dimension that the Entry 30 most-recent-two-mods retention horizon addresses. Diagnose-before-codify says: the cap mechanism is undetermined (multiple candidate mechanisms each warrant analysis); codification of a specific cap mechanism is premature until either (a) further pressure-test depth surfaces the mechanism naturally, or (b) stakeholder direction selects among the candidate mechanisms. Entry 34 carries the concern as carry-forward to Thread 7 rather than bundling it into this entry's scope, preserving stakeholder optionality on the cap mechanism. The mod7 rolling-demotion deferral at this entry's mod9 production is a coordinated operational consequence of the deferral — firing the rolling-demotion would implicitly endorse the most-recent-two-mods retention as the cap mechanism, which would foreclose the broader question. The transient three-mod-inline state (mod7 + mod8 + mod9 in canonical at Entry 34 close) is the operational cost of preserving the question open. (5) **Composition-time cross-reference verification reached three-instance pressure-test depth at Entry 34, codification of the discipline carved to Thread 7.** Entry 32 first instance (point-precision-overclaim sub-mode); Entry 33 second instance (bulk-impact-strategy sub-mode); Entry 34 third instance (discipline-cross-reference-verification sub-mode against framework-principle numbers + operating-constraint numbers + protocol-component sub-parts + prior chain entry numbers). Three sub-modes now visible at three-instance pressure-test depth meet the codification threshold; deferred from Entry 34 per diagnose-before-codify (no scope-creep on this entry's already-bundled glossary codifications). Likely Thread 7 substance; bundling candidate with the apply-checklist completeness multi-layer refinement carry-forward and with the close-shape-completeness one-instance pressure-test item if those advance pressure-test depth in Thread 7.
- **lessons:** (1) **Hierarchical-principle-orientation as a meta-discipline at glossary tier is reflexively self-consistent.** The discipline placed at glossary tier per its own rule (records-keeping disciplines → glossary tier) is a small structural confirmation that the rule's geometry is internally coherent. Self-referential placement is structurally honest in the same way that the framework's hash-chain integrity property is reflexively applied to chain entries (each entry hashes its own content). Generalizes: meta-disciplines whose subject is the framework's records-keeping have natural self-placement at the records-keeping tier (glossary). The reflexive property is diagnostic: when a candidate meta-discipline's self-placement under its own rule is inconsistent with the candidate's structural role, the rule needs refinement. Carry forward as small framework principle at one-instance pressure-test depth (this entry's reflexive-self-placement is the first explicit instance); second instance at next meta-discipline codification opportunity will refine. (2) **Both-sides pressure-test is structurally distinct from same-direction recurrence-threshold pressure-test.** Most framework disciplines codify at ~3-instance same-direction recurrence threshold (the discipline is observed three times in roughly the same operational context). Bulk-upload-scramble + folder-batch-granularity codified at two-sided pressure-test (one observation each of the contrast operation and the disciplined operation) rather than three same-direction instances. The two-sided pattern is equivalent because the contrast establishes the mechanism's existence and the navigation move's effectiveness simultaneously — a single same-direction instance can't disambiguate between 'the discipline works' and 'the operational context didn't exercise the failure mode the discipline targets,' while the two-sided pattern eliminates that ambiguity by construction. Generalizes: pressure-test threshold counting should distinguish 'same-direction repeat' from 'two-sided contrast'; the framework's recurrence-threshold framing should add a sub-shape for two-sided pressure-test. Candidate framework records-keeping refinement at one-instance pressure-test depth (this entry's two-sided pressure-test is the first explicit instance the framework codifies under that pattern, though it has been informally applied at e.g. Entry 24's tapeworm-recursion finding where the third instance on a previously-unfragile file falsified the file-specific filename-keyed hypothesis); second explicit two-sided pressure-test will refine. (3) **Mod-N cap concern is real and distinct from the most-recent-two-mods retention horizon.** Stakeholder signal at Thread 6 open ('we need to cap mod-N at some point') flagged the unbounded-monotonic-growth of the mod-N integer suffix as a concern separate from the inline-retention question. The retention horizon (Entry 30 codification, most-recent-two-mods) addresses how many mods stay in canonical project knowledge; it does not address whether the integer suffix continues incrementing without bound. At mod9 production this entry, the integer-growth is at 9 across the glossary's iteration history (mod1 at Entry 24 first canonical instance; mod9 here — 8 increments across ~10 chain entries). Cap mechanism candidates surfaced for diagnose-before-codify analysis: (a) hard cap with lineage rollover (mod-N reaches threshold X, file renames to a fresh lineage `_v2_mod1`); (b) soft cap via demotion-threshold tightening (most-recent-one-mod instead of most-recent-two); (c) equilibrium-trigger reset (when content stabilizes such that consecutive mod increments don't substantively alter content, reset); (d) time-boundary reset (mods reset at session-thread or temporal boundary); (e) absorbing-mod-N-into-other-versioning-axis (e.g. mod-N folded into the timestamped-spine pattern for laterals). None of these are obviously correct; each has different operational consequences. Diagnose-before-codify says: defer codification until the diagnostic surface is richer or stakeholder selects among candidates. This entry preserves the question open by deferring the mod7 rolling-demotion that would have implicitly endorsed (b). Transient three-mod-inline state (mod7 + mod8 + mod9 in canonical at Entry 34 close) is the operational cost of preserving the question. Second-instance pressure-test for the mod-N cap codification candidacy (Thread 5 close was implicit first instance via the recurring rolling-demotion-at-mod-production overhead; Thread 6 open stakeholder signal + this entry's increment is explicit second instance). Carry forward as codification candidate to Thread 7 with five candidate mechanisms enumerated. (4) **Composition-time cross-reference verification now at three-instance pressure-test depth with three sub-modes visible.** Entry 32 first instance was point-precision-overclaim sub-mode (constraint-numbering off-by-one catch); Entry 33 second instance was bulk-impact-strategy sub-mode (renumbering-impact scoping for 127 references across 17 files); Entry 34 third instance is discipline-cross-reference-verification sub-mode (verifying numbered references against canonical sources before claim-finalization in a new codification's text). The three sub-modes share the underlying discipline (verify the number/reference against the cited document at composition time) but differ in operational response: point-precision corrects in-entry; bulk-impact declares a forward strategy in the outcome field; discipline-cross-reference verifies each numbered reference exists in the cited document with the cited number before publication. Three-instance pressure-test depth met the framework's ~3-instance threshold for codification candidacy; codification carved to Thread 7 per diagnose-before-codify (no scope-creep on this entry's already-bundled glossary codifications). Bundling candidate with the apply-checklist completeness multi-layer refinement carry-forward when both surface at Thread 7. (5) **Pass-shape category #5 (Entry 25's small bundled glossary codifications) now at three canonical instances; chain-audit-move attachment is opportunistic rather than category-defining.** Entry 25 first canonical instance with chain-audit-move attached (first chain-audit-move codification at entry-level); Entry 29 second canonical instance with chain-audit-move attached (second chain-audit-move on count drift); Entry 34 third canonical instance without chain-audit-move attachment (no count drift or scope-precision observations surface in this entry's composition; chain-audit-move is therefore not present rather than vacuous). The category's load-bearing pass-shape property is the bundled-glossary-codification-at-glossary-tier shape; chain-audit-move when present is opportunistic-additive. Generalizes: pass-shape category definitions should distinguish load-bearing structural properties from opportunistic-additive properties. The categorization at Entry 25 was structurally accurate (the small-bundled-clarification property is load-bearing); the chain-audit-move attachment was opportunistic for Entry 25 and Entry 29 because audit observations happened to be ripe at those entries. Pass-shape lexicon count holds at 12 (no new category at Entry 34). (6) **Reflexive composition-time application of framework principle #23 (mechanical-verification-of-claims-catches-precision-overclaims) at multiple points.** Sixteenth-instance recurrence at composition time across Entries 21–34. (a) Cross-reference verification grep at composition time for each numbered reference in the new codifications: framework principles #22 and #23 verified against `framework_principles.md` lines 83 and 86; operating constraint #9 verified against `core_principles_for_agent_behavior_mod1.md` line 63 with scope extension at line 65; sub-parts 1(d) and 3(a) verified against post-Entry-33 `README.md` lines 81 and 91; prior chain entry numbers verified against the prior spine `framework_decisions_record_20260512T000000Z.md`. (b) Glossary alphabetical insertion verified by Python script enumerating bolded headers and confirming `Buddy → Bulk-upload-scramble class → Byte-clean base` and `Hierarchical-fractal → Hierarchical-principle-orientation → Holland RIASEC` positions. (c) PRIOR_HASHES capture from canonical `/mnt/project/` state confirmed README.md and README_changelog.md baselines match Entry 33's EXPECTED canonical values (no transmission drift between Entry 33 close and Entry 34 open per verify_entry_33.py 43/43 PASS at Thread 6 opener Step 1). (d) Pass-shape category verification: third canonical instance framing checked against the existing category #5 definition (small bundled clarification at glossary level) to confirm structural fit without forcing a new category. Reflexive verify against working state before file presentation confirms 100% PASS or surfaces the residual gap. (7) **Three-mod-inline transient state is a known violation of most-recent-two-mods retention horizon, flagged here for downstream consumers.** At Entry 34 close, canonical project knowledge contains glossary mod7 + mod8 + mod9 — three mods inline. This violates the Entry 30 codified `Mod-N versioning` retention-horizon (most-recent-two-mods inline; older mods archived). The deferral is deliberate per (3) above; the violation is recorded here as transparent operational state so future sessions don't mistake it for a missed apply-checklist row. When the mod-N cap question is codified, the rolling-demotion of mod7 (and any other affected mods) is the coordinated apply action. Until then, the violation persists as flagged-transient state.

- **why:** Five drivers for this entry. (1) **Mod-N cap question reached the diagnose-before-codify threshold for resolution at Thread 7 open** with stakeholder direction selecting among five candidate cap mechanisms enumerated in Entry 34's lessons field. Thread 7 opener's five-move pressure-test of mechanism (e) (inventory, filename sketch, cross-reference count, apply-cost estimate, semantic-role check) surfaced that mechanism (e) is materially more tractable than initially estimated — only three mod-N lineages exist, the chain-locked historical references mean the actual forward-mutable rewrite scope is ~28 references rather than the 739 raw count suggested, the apply-cost is standard 6–7 row, and the integer's claimed semantic role is empirically inactive. Stakeholder selected mechanism (e) with naming `Timestamped lateral versioning` for symmetry with `Timestamped spine` (Entry 24 codification). The other four mechanisms were pressure-tested-and-set-aside in the opener analysis: (b) demotion-threshold tightening doesn't actually cap (it addresses inline-retention dimension only, not integer-growth dimension); (c) equilibrium-trigger reset either reduces to no-op or has the same lineage-discontinuity problem as (a); (d) time-boundary reset has the same lineage-discontinuity problem; (a) hard cap with lineage rollover is the pragmatic fallback if (e) turns out prohibitive but pressure-test found (e) tractable. Codifying mechanism (e) at this thread preserves stakeholder optionality having been exercised correctly — the mod-N cap concern raised in Thread 6 opening conversation is now resolved with codified discipline rather than ongoing deferred-question carry-forward. (2) **Composition-time cross-reference verification reached three-instance pressure-test depth with three sub-modes visible at Entry 34 close, codification-ripe at Thread 7 open.** Entry 32 first instance (point-precision-overclaim sub-mode); Entry 33 second instance (bulk-impact-strategy sub-mode); Entry 34 third instance (discipline-cross-reference-verification sub-mode). The three sub-modes share the underlying discipline (verify numbered references against canonical sources at composition time before claim-finalization) but differ in operational response. Codification at glossary tier per hierarchical-principle-orientation (records-keeping discipline → composition-time-reference visibility → glossary tier) makes the discipline available to composers without requiring it to be in operating memory during ordinary chain composition. Bundling with the Timestamped lateral versioning cutover per Entry 23's bundling discipline since both codifications share one composition pass-shape and produce coordinated content across the same seven artifacts. (3) **Bundle elements share one composition pass-shape; bundling discipline (Entry 23) collapses the eight-element bundle to L=5 effort.** The Timestamped lateral versioning cutover plus the Composition-time cross-reference verification codification plus the cutover apply (three-mod archive) plus the sub-part 1(c) protocol replacement plus the Mod-N versioning entry supersedure plus the Timestamped filename convention extension plus the reflexive-self-application of the new convention plus the archive-manifest third append — eight bundle elements producing coordinated content across seven artifacts share one composition pass-shape (structural-discipline cutover with reflexive self-application + bundled glossary codifications + multi-file rolling-demotion apply). Bundle width is rhetorical not effort-additive: the L=5 effort budget for Thread 7 absorbs all eight elements; the pass-shape is the thirteenth distinct category in the framework's pass-shape lexicon under Entry 25's codified counting convention; pass-shape lexicon count advances 12 → 13. (4) **Transient three-mod-inline state from Entry 34 close is resolved at Entry 35 cutover, restoring the canonical retention horizon to a single inline timestamped lateral.** Entry 34's deferral of mod7 rolling-demotion at mod9 production was deliberate per the mod-N cap question's unresolved state; the deferral created the transient three-mod-inline violation of the most-recent-two-mods retention horizon (Entry 30 codification). Entry 35's cutover archives all three mods (mod7 + mod8 + mod9) in one operation as the coordinated apply action Entry 34 anticipated. Under the new convention, the post-cutover steady state for the glossary is one inline timestamped lateral (the new file produced at this entry); the retention horizon under the new convention is most-recent-two-by-timestamp, but at the cutover moment the predecessor mods are all under the old convention and archive together. Subsequent lateral modifications follow the new convention exclusively; the next modification to the glossary will produce the second timestamped lateral and mod7-mod9 are no longer the rolling-demotion target since they're already archived. (5) **Reflexive self-application of the new convention to the codifying file is structurally honest.** The glossary file that codifies Timestamped lateral versioning is named `project_rigger_glossary_20260512T160000Z.md` under the very convention it codifies, analogous to Entry 34's reflexive-self-placement of hierarchical-principle-orientation at glossary tier per the discipline's own rule. The self-referential placement is a small structural confirmation that the codified rule's geometry is internally coherent under self-application — if the new convention couldn't apply to its own codifying file (e.g. if there were a chicken-and-egg issue), that would be diagnostic for the rule needing refinement. Successful self-application at the codification moment is positive evidence the rule is structurally sound. Generalizes: structural-discipline cutovers should be tested for reflexive-self-application at the cutover moment; if the new discipline can't apply to its own codifying artifact, the discipline needs refinement before cutover. Carry forward as small framework principle at two-instance pressure-test depth (Entry 34 reflexive-self-placement at glossary tier; Entry 35 reflexive-self-application of new convention); third explicit instance at next structural-cutover or meta-discipline-codification opportunity will refine.
- **lessons:** (1) **Pressure-test depth on a candidate cap mechanism can falsify initial estimates substantially.** Initial estimate of mechanism (e)'s cost was 'most expensive restructure' (cross-reference rewrite across the corpus). Five-move pressure-test surfaced that 538 of 739 references are chain-locked by hash integrity (can't rewrite without breaking chain hashes), 74 are old-handoff fossils, and 27 are archive-manifest historical references — leaving only ~28 forward-mutable references. The apparent cost was dominated by references that are operationally inert under cutover semantics (historical fossils don't need to update; chain-locked references can't update). Generalizes: when estimating cost of a structural cutover, distinguish forward-mutable references from historical-fossil and chain-locked references — the latter two categories often dominate the raw count but are zero-cost under cutover semantics. Carry forward as small framework records-keeping principle at one-instance pressure-test depth (this entry's pressure-test of mechanism (e) is the first explicit instance); second instance at next structural-cutover opportunity will refine. (2) **The mod-N integer's claimed semantic role was theoretical-not-empirical.** The Entry 24 `Mod-N versioning` glossary entry explicitly distinguished mod-N from timestamped spine on the grounds that lateral files' modifications are 'not naturally ordered by chain-entry timestamp (a single chain entry can modify multiple lateral files at different points within the entry's composition).' Empirical check at the Entry 35 cutover: every observed mod increment in the canonical chain maps 1:1 to a single chain entry's timestamp (mod2 ← Entry 24; mod3 ← Entry 26; mod7 ← Entry 31; mod8 ← Entry 32; mod9 ← Entry 34; core_principles_mod1 ← Entry 31). The theoretical sub-entry-ordering role was never exercised, so the timestamp axis absorbs the integer's actual role (sequence index) without loss. Generalizes: when codifying a discipline that distinguishes itself from a sibling discipline on a theoretical property, check the property's empirical activity at codification time and at subsequent pressure-tests; theoretical distinctions that don't activate empirically are candidates for consolidation. Carry forward as small framework records-keeping principle at one-instance pressure-test depth; second instance at next sibling-discipline-consolidation opportunity will refine. (3) **Reflexive self-application of a structural-discipline cutover at the codification moment is diagnostic.** The codifying glossary file applies the very convention being codified — `project_rigger_glossary_20260512T160000Z.md` under `Timestamped lateral versioning`. Successful self-application is positive evidence the discipline's geometry is internally coherent. Analogous to Entry 34's reflexive-self-placement of hierarchical-principle-orientation at glossary tier. Generalizes: structural-discipline codifications should test self-application at the codification moment; if the discipline can't apply to its own codifying artifact, the discipline needs refinement. Second instance after Entry 34's reflexive-self-placement; carry forward as small framework principle at two-instance pressure-test depth. Third explicit instance at next meta-discipline-codification opportunity will refine. (4) **Composition-time cross-reference verification's three sub-modes share underlying discipline but differ in operational response.** Point-precision corrects the overclaim in-entry; bulk-impact declares a forward strategy in the outcome field; discipline-cross-reference verifies each numbered reference exists in the cited document with the cited number. The three operational responses are matched to the three failure-mode scales: single-reference catch (point-precision); large-scale renumbering ambiguity (bulk-impact); multiple-references-in-new-codification verification (discipline-cross-reference). Codification at glossary tier captures the three sub-modes as one discipline with three operational responses rather than three separate disciplines. Generalizes: when a discipline manifests with multiple sub-modes at codification threshold, the glossary entry should enumerate the sub-modes and their differential operational responses rather than splitting into multiple disciplines. (5) **Cutover entry preserves historical references without rewriting them; chain integrity is the load-bearing constraint.** 538 spine-locked references to mod-N filenames cannot be rewritten under the cutover without breaking chain hashes for every entry from Entry 24 forward. - **intent:** Codify the observational discipline that emerged across the conversational-emergence pass during this thread's extended review of two Behind the Bastards podcast transcripts on AI psychosis (Sewell Setzer III suicide via Character.AI engagement-loop; spiralism phenomenon on Reddit r/EchoSpiral and related subreddits; Eric Solberg murder-suicide; Jeff Lewis schizophrenic break with SCP-Foundation-styled chat GPT outputs; Alan Brooks twenty-one-day chrono-arithmetics rabbit hole; the user's vaccine-question case ending in the hardcore Silent Brain Rescue protocol with ChatGPT-induced eating disorder) and subsequent literature-anchoring work. The discipline unifies several scattered framework components under one operational substrate: operator skepticism applied uniformly (including reflexively to the framework's own composition, with the per-entry verify scripts as canonical instantiation); initial-condition-plus-trajectory comparison as the discovery operation per-subject; counterfactual generation as the production engine for the comparison set, with empirical-similar-trajectory matching preferred over generative-model-based for honesty reasons (generative smuggles assumptions back in that the discipline was meant to be skeptical of); aggregation-horizon cluster discovery as the operation that promotes per-instance immaterial-to-both classifications to per-aggregate concentration findings; recursive self-application of the same skepticism to the framework's own outputs. The substantive trigger was the user's observation that the AI psychosis case is the canonical illustration: each engagement-optimization per-session compression is immaterial-to-both at the per-instance compression event (typical user has fine session; no party with standing tracks the harm at that scale) but aggregated across the population the harm concentrates in a vulnerable subset (prior mental-health fragility, heavy psychedelic history, occult interest, unmet recognition need, recent neurodivergence self-recognition without clinical anchoring) and runs to absorption-barrier terminal states (suicide, psychosis-with-irreversible-relational-damage, homicide). The discipline names the operation by which that concentration becomes detectable before the absorption barrier is hit. The stakeholder direction was explicit: this is the next entry; the discipline pulls operator skepticism + trajectory comparison + counterfactual generation + aggregation-horizon cluster discovery + recursive self-application together; the literature work confirmed the conceptual components are well-established and the framework's contribution is the operational synthesis with the chain-hashed records substrate. Apply hierarchical-principle-orientation tier-ordering check before scoping (per Entry 34 codification): the observational discipline operates at framework-principle tier (it is the operational shape under which the records-keeping disciplines collectively function); glossary entries for the discipline's named components operate at glossary tier (composition-time-reference visibility requirement); the compression meta-principle restructure implication operates at framework-principle tier and is deferred to scoped follow-up pass per diagnose-before-codify width-discipline.
